114. **The sign check CANNOT answer the extensive-vs-intensive `Grad` question, my recommendation to run it was wrong, and the question itself was mis-framed — 2026-09-06.** #113 closed by asking whether `Σ_C`'s extensive `−Grad` is the right way for the term to enter, and I proposed testing it against #71's three-way sign check as the one place `Σ_C`'s form is pinned externally.

    **`Grad` is not in that check.** From `internal notes/_check_sigma_sign.py`, in the source: *“`a_r = −d(Sigma_C)/dR` in every case (P12). **Grad is dropped throughout: it is spherically uninvolved here and identical across the three, so it cannot discriminate.**”* **The run would have produced bit-identical output. Reading the script took under a minute and I should have done it before recommending the check.**

    **Nor can anything else gravitational:** `Grad`'s contribution there is suppressed by `ℓ_ED²/R²`, **`10⁻⁸⁴` to `10⁻¹¹²` across physical scales**, so neither form is measurable in that arc at all. **Nor can the layer-2 Laplacian on a regular lattice:** `n` is constant there, so extensive and intensive differ by an overall constant and give the same operator. **The distinction exists only where `n` varies — an irregular graph, or a growth front. Which is exactly where the probe found it.**

    **So the question was mis-framed, and the right version is different.** Asking whether `Σ_C`'s extensive `−Grad` is *“the right way for the term to enter the functional”* **conflates two things**: **`Σ_C` is a landscape**, and its `Grad` term is the Dirichlet form summed over edges — nothing here challenges that; **how a chain SELECTS among candidates using that landscape is a separate rule.** **The extensive/intensive difference lives entirely in the second, so the functional can stay extensive while selection normalizes — compatible, not competing.** **The correct question is: is ED's commitment-selection rule normalized over the candidate set, or does it use raw `Σ` scores?**

    **And the corpus already answers that, which is the useful part.** **`Paper_003`'s `P-LinRate`** — the load-bearing postulate of the Born-rule derivation — states **`Pr(commit to K*) ∝ b_K* / Σ_K' b_K'`**. **ED's own commitment rule is NORMALIZED over the candidate set: it is a probability, not a raw score.** **So intensive selection is what the corpus already commits to, and the simulator's argmax-over-raw-`Σ` is the outlier** — a deterministic stand-in for a normalized probabilistic rule.

    **A candidate resolution and not a proof, and the gap is stated:** the two normalizations are **not the same object.** `P-LinRate` normalizes over the **channel set at a locus**; the probe normalizes by the **coordination number of the growth front.** **They coincide only if the candidate set is the neighbour set** — true in the probe, **stated nowhere as a general identification.**

    **Net. Withdrawn:** the sign-check recommendation. **Relocated:** the question is about the **commitment rule**, not `Σ_C`'s construction — `Σ_C` is untouched. **Newly connected, and not drawn by either paper: `P-LinRate` already normalizes**, which makes intensive selection the corpus-consistent reading and is **the strongest thing supporting #111's finding** — the form that binds is also the form ED's own selection postulate prescribes. **This links the Born-rule arc to the phase-coherence arc for the first time.** `foundations/Note_ExtensiveGrad_Question_2026-09-06.md`. Census unchanged at 173.
113. **`Grad`'s TIER REVISITED 2026-09-06 — derivation status unchanged, evidential standing materially changed, and one new caveat that stops the upgrade being over-read.** `Grad` has sat at *“proposal, form-forced-conditional at best … four reasons to adopt it provisionally and no reason to call it derived”* since 2026-09-05, and four items have landed since.

    **Still not derived, and that does not move.** Nothing forces the Dirichlet form from P01–P13. **The tier line stands.**

    **What changed is the KIND of support.** The original four are **consistency** reasons — naturalness, a limit check, compatibility, utility. **Two dynamical ones now exist.** **(7) `Grad`'s phase half is the term that BINDS, and the only one that does** (#111): maximised over a candidate's free phase `−Grad` gives exactly `2|acc|`, and **`2|acc|/n` is the one functional tested that raises phase reach above the no-phase control** (**`+81%` at matched connection**, #110), while canonical `Coh` cannot under any normalization tried. **That is a successful prediction of the functional's SHAPE checked against dynamics, not against another formula.** **(8) `Grad` owns the corpus's one measured phase result** (#112) — `Paper_PhaseCoherence_P12Coh` states canonical `Coh` in §1.3 and implements `|acc|/n`, which is `Grad`'s phase half intensively scored.

    **And the recorded problem was never `Grad`'s.** Condition (A)'s crystallization was logged as *“a known problem it did not have when written”*. **#105 showed the asymmetry making it look `Grad`-specific was the `/n`**, and **#106 found canonical `Coh` crystallizes MORE readily — `9/10` seeds against `Grad`'s `4/10` at weak coupling, one-tailed `p ≈ 0.029`.** **So on Knots-safety `Grad` is the better of the two.**

    **The caveat that keeps this honest, and it is not small.** **`Grad` as it appears in `Σ_C = Coh − Str − Grad` is EXTENSIVE, and extensive `Grad` does NOT bind** — its alignment gain is zero to within measurement (#110). **The binding requires INTENSIVE scoring, which is a convention about how the term enters candidate selection, not a property of the functional.** **So supports (7) and (8) are evidence for `Grad`'s PHASE STRUCTURE — the `v`-to-neighbour edge difference with no neighbour–neighbour term — and NOT for its extensive appearance in `Σ_C`.** **Reading them as support for the latter would be an over-reach, and it is exactly the kind this line has produced four times.**

    **Net: “proposal” remains the right word for the derivation. What is no longer accurate is “four reasons and a known problem”** — it is **six reasons, one of them dynamical, with the problem reattributed and a new structural caveat attached.** **And a question this opens rather than closes:** if the term that binds is `Grad` scored intensively, **is `Σ_C`'s extensive `−Grad` the right way for the term to enter the functional at all?** That is a question about `Σ_C`'s construction, it has not been asked before, and nothing here answers it. `foundations/Note_Grad_Proposal_2026-09-05.md` §5.
112. **PAPER NAMING FIXED 2026-09-06 — `Paper_PhaseCoherence_P12Coh` measures `Grad`, not `Coh`, and the canonical attribution in `Paper_087` is corrected.** #111 established that the arm which binds is **intensive `Grad`**. This writes that back to the measured result and its citation sites.

    **The mismatch is between the paper's stated functional and its implemented one.** **§1.3 states the right formula** — `Coh = |Σ_a P_a|² − Σ_a|P_a|² = 2Σ_{a<b}√(b_a b_b)cos Δπ_ab` — **but the build implements `v3_active`, `|acc|/n`.** Maximised over the candidate's free phase the §1.3 formula gives **`2|acc| + (|acc|² − n)`**, carrying a **neighbour–neighbour** cross term that interferes every *pair* of contributions; **`|acc|/n` carries no such term — it is the `v`-to-neighbour part alone over the coordination number, i.e. `Grad`'s phase half intensively scored.** **So the measured object is `Grad`.** **This is an intent/implementation gap, not a preference about naming.**

    **What it costs, tiered.** **(1) The headline SURVIVES and is stronger.** Knots-safety in the physical condition was re-run on **ten seeds across all three functionals — `Grad`, `|acc|/n`, canonical `Coh` — and every one gives 0/10 crystal** (#107), `R ≈ 0.01–0.07` against `R > 0.8`. **So the no-crystal conclusion holds for the formula §1.3 states, not only for the one the build ran.** **(2) The attractive/constructive SIGN transfers** — `+Coh` and `−Grad` both reward alignment, coming from the same amplitude structure and differing only by the NN term. **So `RelationalTick`'s V5 clock-binding and `QuadraticStrain`'s MOND interference term keep the sign this paper was written to supply**, and pointers saying so were added to both. **(3) The FINITE-REACH result does NOT transfer** — the measured `ξ` and the binding it demonstrates are properties of **`Grad` intensively scored**; canonical `Coh` was tested directly and **shows no alignment gain under any normalization tried** (#111), because the NN term does not contain the candidate's own phase.

    **Canonical `Paper_087` §P12 corrected**, which is the propagation site that mattered: it read that `Coh` is operationalized there *“build-verified in 2D and 3D, tier **MEASURED** for finite-reach”*. **The formula stands; the attribution does not.** Now records that **`Coh`'s phase-coherence reading is form-forced-conditional and NOT build-verified — the build verified `Grad`'s** — with the Knots-safety and sign results marked as transferring and the finite-reach one as not.

    **The repair does not weaken the arc.** The paper measures that **`Grad`'s phase content binds with finite reach and stays Knots-safe** — a real result about a real term in `Σ_C`, and now the second independent reason to prefer the `Grad` reading. **What it is not is a measurement of `Coh`.** Files: the paper's preamble, `Paper_087` §P12, `Paper_RelationalTick_v1`, `Paper_QuadraticStrain_v1`. Census unchanged at 173.
111. **REPAIR TESTED 2026-09-06 — binding needs TWO conditions, only `Grad` can meet both, and the July measurement therefore belongs to `Grad`.** #110 proposed entering canonical `Coh` into candidate selection **normalized** — a scoring convention, not a change to the functional.

    **The naive `Coh/n` failed, and the reason names the fix.** `Coh` has two pieces of **different degree in `n`**: when neighbours align `|acc| ~ n` but `|acc|² ~ n²`, **so one factor of `/n` cannot make both intensive** — the quadratic piece stays linear in `n` and keeps chasing crowded cells. The correct form divides each piece by its own degree: **`|acc|/n + (|acc|² − n)/2n²`**.

    **Criterion (a), connection flatness, improves monotonically: canonical `0.480` → naive `Coh/n` `0.320` → intensive `0.145`** (reference `|acc|/n` is `0.066`). **The intensive form removes ~70% of the connection-chasing — that half of the repair works.** **Criterion (b), reach, FAILS: intensive `Coh` runs `ξ = 1.63–1.87`, never past the no-phase control's `2.08`, despite nearly flat connection.**

    **And the failure identifies the cause.** Alignment residuals — reach the connection does not account for — put intensive `Coh` at **`−1.17 … −0.68`, essentially the control's `−1.02`**, against the `|acc|/n` reference's **`+0.70 … +0.97`**. **Intensive `Coh` has NO alignment gain: normalization removed its cost and delivered no benefit.** **So intensivity is necessary and not sufficient, and a clean 2×2 falls out** — **extensive/no-NN (`Grad`): no binding; extensive/with-NN (canonical `Coh`): no binding; intensive/with-NN (intensive `Coh`): no binding; INTENSIVE/NO-NN (`|acc|/n`): BINDS.** **Both conditions are required, and the second is the absence of the neighbour–neighbour term.**

    **There is an established mechanism for that.** #105 showed **the NN term does not contain `v`'s own phase** — it scores how coherent `v`'s *neighbourhood* already is, regardless of what `v` would do. **So it is a reward carrying no information about the choice being made**, biasing selection toward already-coherent pockets rather than toward alignment.

    **The consequence answers the question this whole line opened with.** **`|acc|/n` IS intensive `Grad`** — `Grad` maximised over `v`'s free phase is `2|acc|`, and dividing by `n` leaves the `2` to be absorbed into `k_phase`. **So the arm that binds is not “neither term”; it is `Grad`, entered intensively.** That fills out #104, which correctly said `|acc|/n` is not canonical `Coh`. **So the July phase-alignment measurement belongs to `Grad`** — and **`Coh` cannot reproduce it under any normalization tested, because the very term that distinguishes `Coh` from `Grad` is what kills the binding.** **This is the first thing in the entire `Coh`-vs-`Grad` line that discriminates the two on BEHAVIOUR rather than on artifacts, and it favours `Grad`.**

    **Claim 2 is answered, not merely fixable.** The paper's attractive/constructive sign **is** supplied — by `Grad` intensively scored — **so V5 clock-binding and the MOND interference term keep the support they were promised.** What was never available is the same result from `Coh`. **The corpus should name the July operationalization as `Grad`, intensively scored**, rather than as the simulator's `v3_active` convention.

    **Limits, and they are real.** Three seeds, one grid, one condition, one deposit rule; the 2×2 is clean but each cell is a small sample. **Intensive `Coh`'s connection is flatter than canonical's but still 2.2× the reference's, so criterion (a) is improved rather than met** — some chasing survives the intensive form and is unexplained. **And this is the fourth move in the same direction** (original preference for `Coh` → #105 withdrew the reason → #106 found `Coh` crystallizes more readily → this finds `Coh` cannot bind), each from a methodological fix — **consistent, but this comparison has reversed before.** `foundations/Note_RepairTest_2026-09-06.md`.
110. **EFFECT 2 QUANTIFIED 2026-09-06 — the phase term DOES bind (`+81%` reach at matched connection), so claim 2 is FIXABLE rather than fatal.** #109 identified two effects and measured only one. Swept `k_phase` on every arm tracking `(mean|A|, ξ)` together, condition (C), 48×48, 3 seeds, control re-run at the same grid so everything is internally comparable.

    **Effect 1, fitted across all 13 points: `ξ = 4.79 − 4.68·mean|A|`, `r = −0.844`** — reach falls about **4.7 lattice units per unit of transport connection**.

    **Effect 2, measured DIRECTLY rather than by residual, because two points sit at the same connection.** **Control: `mean|A| = 0.359`, `ξ = 2.08`. Old-“Coh” at `k = 8`: `mean|A| = 0.366`, `ξ = 3.77`.** **Connection matched to within 2%; reach `+1.69` lattice units, an `81%` increase.** **And it holds across the sweep:** at `k = 1,2,4` old-“Coh” runs at `|A| = 0.300–0.315` — **below the control** — while reaching `ξ = 4.09–4.28`. **It gains reach without paying any connection cost at all.**

    **And the canonical terms gain essentially nothing.** **`Grad` at `k=1`:** connection rises `0.359 → 0.421`, and effect 1 alone predicts a reach loss of `4.68 × 0.062 = 0.29`; **observed loss `2.08 → 1.81`, i.e. `0.27`.** **`Grad`'s entire reach loss is the connection — its alignment gain is zero to within measurement.** Canonical `Coh` does slightly better than predicted (`0.75` observed against `1.12`), so it has *some* alignment gain, **but it is swamped, because its connection climbs three times faster.**

    **The artifact I am NOT reporting as a result.** The regression residuals **trend with `k`** for both canonical arms (`Grad` `−1.01 → −0.04`; `Coh` `−0.65 → +0.99`). **That is a saturation artifact:** `ξ` cannot fall below zero, so the true relation flattens at high `|A|` while a straight line keeps descending, under-predicting there and manufacturing positive residuals. **So the matched-connection comparison is the measurement and the residual column is not** — reporting those residuals as arm-specific alignment gains would have been this thread's next artifact.

    **What it settles. Claim 2 is FIXABLE, not fatal.** The phase term **does** supply constructive binding — `+81%` reach at matched connection is not marginal. **What the canonical terms do wrong is not the alignment; it is that they drag the growth front into high-connection territory, and that costs more reach than the alignment gains.** **And the mechanism of the drag is now visible:** `|acc|` and canonical `Coh` reward **agreement × quantity**, and the quantity part chases cells with many committed neighbours, which sit in high-`ρ` pockets where `|A|` is large. **`|acc|/n` rewards agreement QUALITY only, so it does not chase them** — its `|A|` is flat across an 8× sweep.

    **The repair this points to, offered as a proposal and not tested: enter the canonical term into candidate selection in a NORMALIZED form.** That is a **scoring convention**, not a change to `Coh` or `Grad` as functionals — **exactly where #108 and #109 located the problem.** **Whether a normalized canonical `Coh` recovers the binding is the obvious next run and has not been done.** **Limits:** three seeds, one grid (48, smaller than the earlier 64 — control re-run to match, so internal comparisons are sound but cross-note absolute values are not); effect 1's fit has known curvature at the high end; **the `+81%` rests on one matched pair**, supported by the flat-`|A|` trend across the old-“Coh” sweep. `foundations/Note_Effect2_Quantified_2026-09-06.md`.
109. **FAITHFUL REPLAY RUN 2026-09-06 — #108 confirmed by test, and the mechanism is WHERE the front goes, not WHAT SHAPE it is.** #108's replay flattened `ρ` and its numbers were discarded. This one records, for **every deposit**, the exact `(neighbour, A)` pairs that entered the calculation, with `A = κ_bw(b_wv − 1) + κ_ρ(ρ_w − ρ_v)` and **`ρ` accumulating with every commit** — so the replay is bit-faithful given the order.

    **Validation is EXACT in all four arms.** Condition (C), 64×64, 5 seeds: control `ξ` live **2.13** / replay **2.13**; `Grad` **0.83 / 0.83**; canonical `Coh` **0.73 / 0.73**; old-“Coh” **3.83 / 3.83**. **So #108's claim is now tested rather than read: the phase field is fully determined by the commit order plus the connection values, with no dependence on which arm's bonus produced that order.** The `ξ` differences are entirely growth, not phase arithmetic.

    **And the obvious explanation is RULED OUT.** **Front coordination is identical across every arm** — `1.94–1.97` committed neighbours at deposit. **So it is not about how many neighbours a cell has when it commits**, which was the natural guess. **What differs is the connection the front encounters:** `mean|A|` is **0.362** (control), **0.364** (old-“Coh”), **0.844** (`Grad`, 2.3×), **1.076** (canonical `Coh`, 3.0×); `std(A)` runs `0.265 / 0.388 / 0.603 / 0.744`. **`A` is what phases are transported through, and the two arms with the largest `|A|` have the shortest reach.** **So the story is not “a different shape” but “the same shape in a different place”: the phase bonus steers the front into territory with large `ρ`-differences between neighbours, which widens the transport connection, which shortens the reach. Nothing about the phase FORMULA is involved.**

    **What is NOT explained, and it is the interesting residue.** **`mean|A|` does not explain everything: the control and old-“Coh” have essentially identical connections (`0.362` vs `0.364`) yet `ξ = 2.13` against `3.83` — same transport, nearly double the reach.** **So there are TWO effects and only one is identified:** **(1) the connection effect** — canonical terms triple `|A|` and lose reach, identified; **(2) a genuine alignment effect** — old-“Coh” raises reach at *unchanged* connection, **not explained here**, and presumably the phase reward doing what the paper says it does, applied without the coordination-number bias the extensive forms carry. Correlation `std(A)` vs `ξ` across the four arms is `r = −0.714` — right sign, loose **precisely because old-“Coh” is the outlier effect (2) accounts for.**

    **Where the arc stands.** **Unchanged:** the Knots result (#107) and the substance of claim 2 — both canonical terms leave the physical substrate at `ξ < 1`, and no reach means no binding. **Now established:** **why** — the growth rule steering the front into high-`|A|` territory, **so the repair, if there is one, belongs to how the phase bonus enters CANDIDATE SELECTION, not to `Coh` or `Grad`.** **Now visible and new:** **old-“Coh” binds at unchanged connection**, the one arm doing what the paper claims the phase term does — **a reason to look harder at the `/n`**, not as the canonical term it is not, but as **the only form so far that supplies binding without the growth-side cost.** **Limits:** five seeds, one grid, one condition; `r` on four points is an illustration, not a statistic; **effect (2) is unquantified.** `foundations/Note_FaithfulReplay_2026-09-06.md`.
108. **The `ξ` drop is SHAPE, not phase disagreement — and the argument needs no simulation.** #107 found both canonical terms driving `ξ` below the no-phase control in the physical case, and flagged a confound it could not resolve: the probe **picks which cell commits next** *and* **lays down that cell's phase**, and the phase term enters both, so a falling `ξ` could be **(a)** phases genuinely disagreeing or **(b)** a differently-shaped blob with different phase statistics.

    **Cause (a) was never available, and reading the source settles it.** **In every arm of every probe in this line, `deposit(v)` takes the mean-field angle of the committed neighbours — the arm does not enter it.** `Grad`, `coh_v3` and canonical `Coh` differ **only** in the bonus used to pick the next candidate. **So two arms handed the same growth order produce bit-identical phase fields, and the entire arm-to-arm `ξ` difference is which cells committed in which order.** **That is a fact about the code, not a result about ED**, and it resolves the confound: **the drop is shape.**

    **The probe confirms the mechanism.** Depositing phases along different growth orders under an identical rule, condition (C): **control order `ξ = 1.30, 1.30, 1.34` against `Coh`(k=8) order `ξ = 1.58, 1.56, 1.69`** across three seeds. **Order alone moves `ξ`.**

    **But the probe's ABSOLUTE numbers are flagged and not used, because my replay flattens `ρ`.** The live connection carries `κ_ρ(ρ_w − ρ_v)` where **`ρ` accumulates with every commit**; the replay sets `ρ = 1` on commit and `0` before, **removing most of the `ρ`-disorder — which is the dominant part of the connection in condition (C).** **So the replay's `ξ ≈ 1.3–1.6` are for a much less disordered connection than the live run's, and its apparent direction (canonical arms ABOVE the control) cannot be compared with the live direction (they were below).** It is faithful about *ordering effects existing*, not about their size or sign. **Recorded plainly because reporting those numbers as the answer would have been this thread's fifth artifact.**

    **What this does to claim 2 — substance unchanged, description changed, and the change is the useful part.** **Unchanged:** in the physical condition both canonical terms leave the substrate at **`ξ < 1`** at strong coupling, the paper's own *“no binding at all”*. **A substrate with no phase reach supplies no constructive binding whatever produced it**, so the problem for the two downstream results citing that sign — **V5 clock-binding and the MOND interference term** — stands exactly as #107 stated it. **Changed:** the mechanism is **not** *“the canonical terms scramble phases”* but *“they steer growth into a front whose phase reach is short”*. **Those call for different repairs — the first would be a problem with the phase functional, the second is a problem with what the phase bonus does to CANDIDATE SELECTION, i.e. with the `Σ`-scoring rather than with `Coh` or `Grad` as functionals.** **So the live problem moves off the `Coh`-versus-`Grad` question entirely.**

    **Owed:** a **faithful replay** preserving the live `ρ` trajectory, which would give the real size and direction of the shape effect. Until then §3 establishes **that** shape matters and not **how much**. **Untouched:** the Knots result (#107 — 0/10 crystal, ten seeds, all readings), which is measured on the live dynamics. `foundations/Note_FixedMorphology_2026-09-06.md`; `../event-density/theory/p12_fixed_morphology_probe.py`.
107. **CONDITION (C) MULTI-SEEDED 2026-09-06 — the July Knots result is ROBUST, and a different, more consequential problem surfaces in the `ξ` column.** Ten seeds on the **physical** case (quenched bandwidth **and** `ρ` holonomy), with a `k_phase = 0` control.

    **Framing first, because #103–#106 reported these numbers without saying it: CRYSTALLIZATION IS THE FAILURE MODE.** *“Knots, Not Crystals”* — the certified substrate has no long-range ordering coupling. **And total disorder is also failure**, since `Paper_PhaseCoherence_P12Coh` exists to supply the **attractive/constructive sign** V5's clock-binding and the MOND interference term assumed. **The target is the middle — finite reach**, and §5.4 states the window: too weak *“crystallizes”*, too strong gives *“`ξ < 1`: complete decorrelation, **no binding at all**”*.

    **The good news, and it is real. `Paper_PhaseCoherence_P12Coh`'s headline holds across ten seeds and all three readings: 0/10 crystal in EVERY arm at EVERY coupling.** `R ≈ 0.01–0.07` throughout against `R > 0.8` for a crystal verdict — **not a near miss.** That claim was resting on one seed; **it now has ten and survives all of them.**

    **The problem is in the `ξ` column, not the verdict column.** Against the `k_phase = 0` control (**`ξ = 2.1`** — the right baseline, since phases are still deposited but do not influence which candidate wins): **old-“Coh” `|acc|/n` goes `2.1 → 4.1` — it RAISES correlation, it BINDS. `Grad` goes `2.1 → 1.8 → 0.8`. CANONICAL `Coh` goes `2.1 → 1.3 → 0.7`.** **Both canonical terms sit BELOW the no-phase control at every coupling tested, and both fall to `ξ < 1` by `k_phase = 8` — which is the paper's own stated failure, “no binding at all”.** **So there is no coupling at which either canonical term improves phase correlation over having no phase term.** **The one arm that supplies the constructive binding the paper was written to establish is `|acc|/n` — which #104 showed is neither `Coh` nor `Grad`, but `Grad/n`.**

    **What it does NOT say, held honestly.** **Not that the canonical terms are wrong.** `ξ` is measured on a growth morphology, and the phase bonus changes **which sites get selected**, not only how phases align — **a term biasing growth toward high-`|acc|` sites changes the front's geometry, and some of the `ξ` change is that rather than de-correlation per se.** Separating the two is not done here and is the obvious follow-up. **And it does not settle `Coh` vs `Grad`:** both go down, with `Coh` down harder, **consistent with #106's finding that the neighbour–neighbour term adds alignment drive.**

    **Where it leaves the arc — two claims now SEPARATED, which they were not before.** **(1) Knots-safety in the physical case: ROBUST**, ten seeds, every reading, stronger than it was. **(2) The attractive/constructive sign: holds ONLY for `|acc|/n`.** Since that functional is neither canonical term, **the sign that the V5 clock-binding and MOND-interference results were promised is currently supplied by an operationalization the corpus has no other reason to prefer.** **Claim 2 is the live problem, it is a DIFFERENT problem from the one this line has chased since 2026-09-05, and it is more consequential — two downstream results cite that sign.** **Claim 1 is a confirmation and is reported as one; claim 2 is a negative, found by adding a control and ten seeds to a run that previously had neither.** `foundations/Note_CondC_MultiSeed_2026-09-06.md`; `../event-density/theory/p12_condA_multiseed.py C`.
106. **CONDITION (A) MULTI-SEEDED 2026-09-06 — the neighbour–neighbour term is NOT inert, and #105 is revised: canonical `Coh` orders at WEAKER coupling than `Grad`.** Everything in #103–#105 rested on **one seed**. Ten seeds, each re-drawing the quenched disorder, the start node and the deposition randomness — independent disorder realizations, not just different growth orders on one lattice.

    **The control clears the ground first, and without it nothing below is attributable.** At **`k_phase = 0`** — phases deposited but with no influence on which candidate wins — **`R = 0.10 [0.02, 0.22]`, `ξ = 6.0`, CRYSTAL 0/10.** **The lattice and deposit rule alone produce disorder**, so (A)'s crystallization is genuinely caused by the phase term and **#105's re-attribution survives its first real test.**

    **The result, and it is not what one seed showed.** At **`k_phase = 8`**: `Grad` **10/10**, canonical `Coh` **10/10** — identical, as the single seed suggested. **At `k_phase = 1` they are not: `Grad` CRYSTAL 4/10 (`R = 0.70`), canonical `Coh` CRYSTAL 9/10 (`R = 0.86`).** **One-tailed Fisher exact `p ≈ 0.029`** (two-tailed `0.057`). The old `|acc|/n` arm is **0/10 at both couplings**, robustly finite-reach.

    **The one-tailed test is the right one because the direction was PREDICTED before the run, not read off it.** `Coh = −Grad + 2·NN`, and **the neighbour–neighbour term is an ADDITIONAL alignment reward** — it pays for neighbours agreeing with *each other*, on top of the `v`–neighbour agreement both terms pay for. **A larger alignment drive should order at weaker coupling. It does.** **So the two terms differ in their ordering THRESHOLD, and the difference has a mechanism rather than being a fitted observation.**

    **What this revises.** **#105's *“different objects, same behaviour”* was the single seed talking.** Corrected: **same behaviour at strong coupling; different ordering thresholds at weak coupling, with `Coh` ordering earlier.** #104 measured NN at **29–41% of the phase score** and #105 found it changed no verdict; **on ten seeds it changes the verdict in half the weak-coupling cases.** **This is the first genuine behavioural discriminator between the two terms** — after the Knots draw, the withdrawn `ξ` trend (#103) and the mis-specified comparison (#104) — **and it came from removing a methodological flaw rather than from a new idea, which is the pattern of this whole line.**

    **The uncomfortable part, recorded deliberately. The Knots position on this comparison has moved THREE times in two days:** **(1) originally** `Coh` clean in (A) and `Grad` crystallizing — a reason to prefer `Coh`; **(2) #105** both crystallize equally — reason withdrawn, concern re-attributed to the operationalization; **(3) now** **`Coh` crystallizes MORE readily than `Grad`**, so if (A)-crystallization counts against a reading **it counts harder against `Coh`.** **Each move followed from fixing a real methodological flaw, and each reversed the previous conclusion.** That is the process working — **and it is also a warning: this comparison has been unusually good at producing confident wrong answers.** The current one rests on ten seeds, one grid size, one deposit rule and a `p` of `0.03`. **Do not treat it as settled.**

    **Untouched:** the **physical case (C)**, which is the condition `Paper_PhaseCoherence_P12Coh`'s headline rests on. All three arms were finite-reach there on the single seed. **Multi-seeding (C) is the obvious next check and has not been done.** `foundations/Note_CondA_MultiSeed_2026-09-06.md`; `../event-density/theory/p12_condA_multiseed.py`.
105. **CANONICAL `Coh` BEHAVES LIKE `Grad` — the two-line fix run 2026-09-06, and the distinctive behaviour belonged to NEITHER term.** #104 found the earlier probe's *“Coh reading”* (`|acc|/n`) was `Grad` divided by the coordination number. **This is the corrected run — one arm changed, grid, seed, deposit rule and normalization all held fixed.** Canonical `Coh` maximised over `v`'s free phase is **`2|acc| + (|acc|² − n)`** against `Grad`'s **`2|acc|`**, so **`Coh − Grad = |acc|² − n`, exactly the neighbour–neighbour term.**

    **The baseline reproduces, so the comparison is clean.** 64×64, single seed, `k_phase` swept 16×. **Condition (A), bandwidth holonomy only:** `Grad` `R≈0.89–0.96, ξ=30` **CRYSTAL**; old-“Coh” `R≈0.04–0.19, ξ≈4.2–5.0` finite; **CANONICAL `Coh` `R≈0.87–0.97, ξ=30` CRYSTAL.** **Condition (C), the physical case:** `Grad` finite, `ξ` **2.2→0.8**; old-“Coh” finite, `ξ` **flat 4.2→3.7**; **CANONICAL `Coh` finite, `ξ` 1.6→0.7.** **The `Grad` and old-“Coh” arms match the earlier run exactly**, so any difference in the third arm is the neighbour–neighbour term and nothing else.

    **Canonical `Coh` tracks `Grad` in both conditions. There is no discriminator because there is no difference in behaviour to discriminate.** **And the neighbour–neighbour term is large but inert** — the multi-channel run (#104) measured it at **29–41% of the phase score**, so the two are genuinely different numbers, **yet it changes no verdict anywhere.** **Different objects, same behaviour.**

    **The distinctive signature belonged to NEITHER term.** What made the old arm look like a live alternative — **finite-reach in (A), and `ξ` flat at `≈ 4` across the sweep** — **is a property of `|acc|/n`, which is neither canonical `Coh` nor the `Grad` proposal.** It is `Grad/n`, and #103 showed the `/n` is the growth-front coordination number.

    **Consequences, tiered.** **(a) The question is MOOT AS POSED.** It asked which term owns the July phase-alignment measurement: **in (A) NEITHER does** (both canonical terms crystallize where the measurement is finite-reach); **in (C), the physical case, BOTH do and they are indistinguishable.** The answer is not “one of them” but **“both where it matters, neither where it doesn't.”** **(b) The condition-(A) Knots concern MOVES AND GROWS.** `Note_PhaseInGrad_Probe` recorded (A)-crystallization as **`Grad`'s known problem** with the `Coh` reading clean there — **that asymmetry was the `/n`.** Written canonically, **(A) crystallizes under both**, so it is not a defect of the `Grad` proposal specifically but of **the phase-coherence operationalization in that condition.** **(c) What survives untouched, and is strengthened: the physical case.** **All three arms are finite-reach in (C)**, so `Paper_PhaseCoherence_P12Coh`'s headline — finite-reach rather than crystalline order in the physical condition — **now holds for the canonical terms and not only for the probe's convention.** That is the result the July arc actually rests on. **(d) `Grad`'s tier is unchanged, but its standing relative to `Coh` improves:** the one recorded reason to prefer the `Coh` reading — that `Grad` crystallized in (A) and `Coh` did not — **was an artifact and is withdrawn.**

    **Honest limits.** Single seed, one grid, one deposit rule; the sweep is wide and the verdicts stable across it, but nothing here is a multi-seed statistic. **And one small checkable point rather than an assumption:** all three arms deposit the mean-field angle, which is `Grad`-optimal by construction — **and it is `Coh`-optimal too, because the neighbour–neighbour term does not contain `v`'s phase at all.** That is why one deposit rule serves all three. `foundations/Note_CanonicalCoh_Run_2026-09-06.md`; `../event-density/theory/p12_coh_canonical_probe.py`.
104. **The `Coh`-vs-`Grad` comparison was NEVER ACTUALLY BEING RUN — the probe's `Coh` arm was a normalization of `Grad`. Found 2026-09-06 while building the multi-channel probe #103 asked for.**

    **Writing both formulas out per channel makes the difference exact.** With `v`'s own contribution included and neighbours transported by the P05 connection, **`Coh = −Grad + 2·NN + const`**, where **NN is the neighbour–neighbour cross term** `2Σ_KΣ_{w<w'}√(b_w b_w')cos Δπ_ww'`. **The two terms differ by exactly one object, and the asymmetry is structural: a Dirichlet form only ever compares `v` to a neighbour, never two neighbours to each other.** `Coh` interferes every pair of contributions; `Grad` cannot.

    **And that is where the finding fell out.** `p12_phase_in_grad_probe.py`'s own docstring: *“`v3_active` (**Coh reading**, INTENSIVE): `bonus = |acc|/n` … the substantive difference is **precisely the `/n` normalisation**.”* **`|acc|` is correct for `Grad`** — maximising the Dirichlet form over `v`'s free phase gives exactly `2|acc|`. **But `|acc|/n` is NOT canonical `Coh`**: it is the simulator's `v3_active` per-locus-average convention, and **canonical `Coh` maximised over `v`'s phase gives `2|acc|` PLUS the NN term**, which `|acc|/n` does not contain. **`|acc|/n` is `Grad` divided by the coordination number.**

    **So the comparison being run was `Grad` against `Grad/n`. The question was never asked** — and that explains all three dead ends at once. **Knots-safety drew because both arms were the same functional.** **#103's `ξ` trend was a coordination-number artifact because `/n` IS the coordination number**, and it was the only difference between the arms. **And no tuning helped because there was nothing to tune toward.** **This is the failure mode the corpus already names — testing a hand-built stand-in rather than the thing itself — and it sat undetected because the stand-in had a plausible name.**

    **The multi-channel run itself is new data, not a resolution, and its caveat outweighs its numbers.** 40×40, 3 channels per locus, both formulas as written: **NN carries 29–41% of the phase score**, so `Coh` and `Grad` are genuinely different objects numerically and not just notationally — **but they give the same verdict in every condition tested** (both crystallize in (A), both finite-reach in (C)). **The caveat: this run cannot be compared to the earlier one**, since the earlier one tested a different functional and this probe also changed grid size and added per-channel quenched bandwidth. **`ξ ≈ 0.7` in condition (C) is far shorter than anything previously measured and may be the extra disorder rather than the terms. Single seed, one grid size — do not build on these numbers.**

    **Status.** **Established without any probe: the algebra**, and **the fact about the earlier probe's source code.** **Not established:** which term owns the July phase-alignment measurement — **that question is now genuinely open for the first time, having previously been open *and* mis-specified.** `Grad`'s tier is unchanged (proposal, form-forced-conditional at best); its condition-(A) problem now appears under **both** readings here, which neither confirms nor clears it since the probe is unvalidated. **Next, and it is cheap: re-run the ORIGINAL single-phase probe with its `Coh` arm corrected to `|acc|² − n` rather than `|acc|/n`** — a two-line change that keeps the validated baseline everywhere else and would be **the first run actually asking the question.** `foundations/Note_MultiChannel_CohGrad_2026-09-06.md`; `../event-density/theory/p12_multichannel_coh_grad_probe.py`.
103. **The `Grad` phase discriminator is DEAD 2026-09-06 — it was measuring the growth front — and the reason says what a working probe would need.** The seam's third carried item. `Note_PhaseInGrad_Probe` had come back a draw on Knots-safety and offered one surviving discriminator, **`ξ`'s response to coupling strength**: flat (`≈ 4`) for `Coh`, monotonically shrinking (`2.2 → 0.8`) for `Grad`. **It also flagged its own limit, and that limit was the whole story:** *“the extensive form conflates two effects: alignment quality and coordination number. A cleaner probe would decompose `|acc| = n × (|acc|/n)` and sweep them separately.”* **That probe is now run.**

    **Since `|acc| = n·(|acc|/n)` exactly, the two readings differ by ONE factor of the coordination number.** So: `bonus = n^α·(|acc|/n)` with `α = 0` the `Coh` reading and `α = 1` the `Grad` one, **plus the control that decides it — `bonus = n`, rewarding coordination number with the phase information deleted.** Condition (C), 64×64, matched RNG: **`Coh` `4.2→3.7` (flat, reproducing the earlier run), interpolated `1.8→1.2`, `Grad` `2.2→0.8` (also reproducing it), and the PHASE-BLIND control `2.0 → 0.6`.** **The control tracks `Grad` closely with no phase content whatsoever. The shrinkage is a coordination-number artifact of extensivity, and the discriminator is dead** — the question returns to the draw it was in before the trend was proposed. Withdrawn in both places it was recorded.

    **The more useful half, and it is structural rather than a tuning problem.** **The two terms are extensive in DIFFERENT things:** canonical `Coh` is `2Σ_{a<b}√(b_a b_b)cos Θ_ab`, **a sum over pairs of contributions AT A LOCUS** (in the MOND case: local and horizon, `Paper_030`'s bilocal); `Grad` is `Σ_K Σ_⟨u,v⟩ |P_K(v) − P_K(u)|²`, **a sum over GRAPH EDGES between loci.** **Different index sets — one over sources of participation at a place, the other over places.** **And the probe carries one phase per node**, which collapses “contributions at a locus” and “neighbouring loci” onto the same neighbour set, **so the two sums are identical by construction and the only thing left to differ is the normalization — the factor of `n` that just turned out to be a growth-front effect.** **That is why Knots-safety drew and why the `ξ` trend was spurious: the probe has no degree of freedom in which the two terms differ.**

    **So what a discriminating probe needs is now stated: MULTIPLE CHANNELS PER LOCUS**, each with its own `b_K` and `π_K`, so that **`Coh` reads within-locus pairs and `Grad` reads across-edge differences of each channel separately.** Those come apart immediately — a configuration with strong within-locus alignment and weak across-edge alignment scores high on one and low on the other — **and the current single-phase probe cannot represent that configuration at all.**

    **What does not change:** `Grad` remains a **proposal**, `form-forced-conditional at best`, and its known problem stands untouched (condition (A) crystallizes under the `Grad` assignment — the real finding of the 2026-09-05 run). Which term owns the July phase-alignment measurement is **still open**, and the `cos Θ` discharge still cites that measurement as one of its two supports. **Held to the same bar as a positive: this is a NEGATIVE.** It removes a false lead rather than adding a result — **and its value is that the false lead was sitting in the corpus recorded as live.** `foundations/Note_GradPhase_Decomposed_2026-09-06.md`; `../event-density/theory/p12_grad_decomposed_probe.py`.
102. **`Coh` IS EVALUATED AT LAYER 1, on raw polarity — the seam's second debt paid 2026-09-06, and an error of mine from yesterday corrected on the way.** With #101 this closes **both** live debts of the layer seam, which was Path A's #1 item and the only one feeding four arcs.

    **The correction first, because everything downstream of it moved.** Yesterday's `Note_Theta12_LayerQuestion` wrote *“`Θ₁₂` decorrelates beyond `ξ ≈ 5ℓ_ED`”*. **Wrong, and it is this session's recurring mistake — a number lifted out of its context.** `Paper_PhaseCoherence_P12Coh`'s `ξ ≈ 8–12` and `ξ < 1` are **measurements in lattice units of a 60×60 probe grid**, whose job is §5.4's point that the connection strength has a **usable window**. **The paper's actual statement is preamble 4: `ξ` is identified with V5's memory length `ℓ_V5` and the MOND transition reach, and is value-inherited — “form-forced-finite, value-inherited.”** **So the probe establishes the FORM (finite rather than infinite reach), not the SCALE, and `ξ` is macroscopic (`≳ 10¹⁵ m` even for the Sun), not five substrate lengths.**

    **The answer, in four steps each citing something prior.** **(1)** `a_C = −∇_adj Σ_C`, and `∇_adj` is *“a difference over substrate-graph adjacency, not a continuum gradient”* — **accelerations are definitionally layer-1 objects** (§4f, the same commitment #101 leaned on) — **so `Coh`, a term inside `Σ_C`, is evaluated at layer 1.** **(2)** Layer-1 polarity is **raw polarity transported by P05's connection**; the coarse-grained holonomy is layer 2 by construction. **(3)** That connection is not flat (`Paper_PhaseCoherence` §4), and its quenched disorder gives `Coh` a **finite reach `ξ`** rather than crystalline order. **(4)** **`Coh` is the MOND term** — #71's three-way sign check placed the bilocal cross term in **`+Coh`**.

    **Steps 3 and 4 close a loop with preamble 4: `Coh` is the MOND term; `Coh` at layer 1 has reach `ξ`; `ξ` IS the MOND transition reach.** Three statements made independently in three places, **consistent only if `Coh` sits at layer 1.**

    **And the counterfactual is what makes it decisive rather than merely tidy.** A layer-2 `Coh` is phase-averaged by construction — which is the layers program's own account of what the seam does — so `cos Θ` is **0 everywhere** (the bilocal cross term vanishes, **no `√(a_N a₀)` at all**) or **1 everywhere** (a cross term with **no transition**, i.e. MOND in the solar system). **Either way the MOND transition scale loses its origin in `Σ_C`. Layer 2 is not merely unmotivated; it removes the thing that gives `a₀` its reach.**

    **What it does NOT do.** **It does not derive `ξ`** — the paper is explicit that the reach is value-inherited, and nothing here changes that. **It says WHERE the MOND scale enters `Σ_C` — through `Coh`'s finite reach at layer 1 — not what the scale is.** **And it inherits the operationalization's own tier:** preamble 2 says reading `Coh` as phase-coherence is *“form-forced conditional on the reading”* and that **the certified `Σ`-rule's `Coh` is `−(ρ−ρ_*)²`, phase-blind.** **This settles which layer the phase-coherence reading lives at; it does not promote that reading.**

    **Consequence for `Θ₁₂`: the fork collapses and both horns now agree.** Yesterday's fork was *controllable at layer 2, separation-dependent at layer 1*. **Layer 1 wins — but with `ξ` corrected, layer 1 delivers the layer-2 answer at any laboratory scale:** `Θ₁₂` is a raw-polarity phase difference whose decorrelation length is the MOND reach (`≳ 10¹⁵ m`), and a collapse experiment spans metres, **so `Θ₁₂` is coherent across any realizable branch separation — controllable.** **The FORM of yesterday's layer-1 statement was right; the SCALE was wrong by about fifteen orders of magnitude**, which flips the practical answer from *“decorrelates in any real experiment”* to *“decorrelates in none”*. **That is a materially different prediction for the collapse arc, and it is this item's most useful output.** `layers/Note_CohLayer_Settled_2026-09-06.md`.
101. **THE `2π` DIRECTION IS SETTLED 2026-09-06 — the seam divides because layer 1 COUNTS and layer 2 WINDS, and that layer 1 counts is P11.** Path A's #1 open item, and the debt §4f had narrowed to a single sentence: *given that we compare at layer 1, why does the seam contribute `1/2π` rather than `2π`?* — noting that **dimensional analysis does not fix it**, both readings being legal and differing by `(2π)² ≈ 39`.

    **What the `2π` in `T = κ/2π` actually is, and it is not decoration:** it is `ω = 2πν`. In the Euclidean-period derivation the inverse temperature **is** the period, `β = 2π/κ`, **so `κ` is radians per unit time (an `ω`) and `T = 1/β` is cycles per unit time (a `ν`).** Dividing by `2π` converts one to the other and **nothing else is happening in that factor.** **So “divide or multiply” is really: is the chain's side an `ω` or a `ν`?**

    **§4b had already answered it without noticing.** *“A chain's acceleration is, in ED, a P11 commitment-rate asymmetry … That is a **bare count-rate** at the level of commitments.”* **A count-rate is a `ν`** — P11's operational content is *“discrete … irreversible substrate events”*, and **discrete events are counted, which is cycles per second, not radians per second.** **And the horizon's side is an `ω`:** it has **no layer-1 `κ`** (`Paper_028` §3.4 makes the cosmic surface statistical, the qualifier §4f added) and presents only coarse-grained as de Sitter-like with `κ_H = H₀`. **So the comparison is `ν` against `ω`, one conversion, in the direction that divides: `a₀ = c·T_H = cH₀/2π`.** **Multiplying instead would require the chain's acceleration to be a continuous phase advance rather than a count of discrete events — exactly what P11 denies.**

    **And it explains a pattern the note had observed without a cause.** §4b predicted that every comparison of a bare chain rate to a horizon thermal state carries **exactly one** `2π` and every like-layer comparison carries **none**; §4c measured precisely that (`𝒲₀` against `(cH₀)²` — *“the π vanishes entirely”*). **Cross-layer is `ν`-against-`ω` and needs one conversion; like-layer needs none. The pattern came first and now has a cause, which is the right order.**

    **Tier, and the circularity I deliberately did not use.** This is a **type argument** — about what *kind* of quantity sits on each side — **not a computation.** **Stronger than §4b's** *“the chain is what responds”* because it cites **P11's discreteness** instead of an intuition; **weaker than a derivation of `a₀`**, which nobody has. **Deliberately NOT used as evidence: that only the dividing reading lands near the measured value** (`1.04×10⁻¹⁰` vs `1.2×10⁻¹⁰`, against `4.1×10⁻⁹` for multiplying). **Matching `a₀` is what the route is trying to explain, so using the match to pick the direction would be circular** — the arithmetic is in `internal notes/_check_twopi_direction.py` to show the stakes, not to make the case.

    **What closes and what does not.** **Closed:** §4f's owed sentence, which asked for the **direction**. **Not closed:** the **value** — `a₀ ~ cH₀` stays form-derived with the scale inherited from `H₀`, and per #60 **`a₀ ~ cH₀` is Milgrom's (1983) with the `2π` form standard in the MOND review literature**; ED's claim is the coefficient's *origin*, and this gives that origin's direction, not a new number. **And for the layers program specifically: this is the FIRST numerical factor the seam has been shown to carry** — `layers/Note_TheSeam_And_SigmaC` §4 had recorded that the seam *“is not understood as a carrier of numerical factors”*, and that sentence is now qualified rather than true: **it carries *this* one, by a type distinction; it remains uncharacterized for quantities that are neither count-rates nor angular rates.** **Falsifier:** show commitment is a continuous phase advance rather than a count of discrete events, and the chain's side becomes an `ω`, the comparison becomes like-layer, and **the `2π` vanishes** — giving `a₀ = cH₀`, `5.5×` the measured value. `gravity/Note_a0_TwoPi_RepairRoutes` §4h.
100. **Two bankings from an external mass reading 2026-09-06, and two answers on the `√2` and the 3s — with the rest deliberately NOT banked.** An external model offered a mass-sector reading in ED's idiom. **Three of its four claims are already corpus content** (`√m` as the substrate amplitude = `Paper_113` + the carrier `P = √b e^{iπ}`; the 120° generation modes = the Brannen form in `Note_MassArc_Koide_CoherenceReframe`; and `Σm = 4Σ√(mᵢmⱼ)` = **ledger #81's `Coh = ½Str` verbatim**). **Recorded as not-banked on purpose — re-banking existing results under a new name is how a corpus ends up citing itself through a third party.**

    **BANKED 1 — `P-Commitment-Linear` reconciles the amount-reading and the rate-reading of mass.** `Paper_113` gives mass as rest-frame **bandwidth content** (an *amount*); the external reading gives it as a **commitment rate** (`ω = mc²/ℏ` literal). **That is the same amount-vs-rate axis that forced propagation from P04 to P05 in #96.** But `Paper_GR-I` §2's **`P-Commitment-Linear`** — `Γ_commit ∼ b_int/reserve` — bridges them: **`m ∝ Γ_commit × reserve`.** **The rate-reading is not a rival to `Paper_113`; it is the same quantity from the other side, via a postulate ED already has.** **The cost, unstated in the source: the proportionality needs a COMMON COMMITMENT RESERVE ACROSS GENERATIONS** — and if the reserve differs between `e`, `μ`, `τ` the two readings come apart **exactly where the generations differ, which is the only place the identification is asked to work.** **Tier: a stated bridge between two posits, conditional on a common reserve.**

    **BANKED 2 — `Q = 2/3` is the midpoint in `Q` ALONE, so “equilibrium” arguments from it are parametrization artifacts.** The endpoints are right (`1/3` degenerate, `→1` disjoint) but the centrality is not: **`Q` midpoint `0.667` — leptons `0.667` ✓; `w` range `[0,2]` midpoint `1.000` — leptons `1.414` ✗; `CV` range `[0,√2]` midpoint `0.707` — leptons `1.000` ✗.** **A midpoint is not distinguished unless something selects the parametrization, and nothing does.** **Second parametrization artifact in this material in one day** — the first being that **the `√2` in `w = √2` is itself a Brannen-normalization artifact** (`Σcos² = 3/2` over three 120° points).

    **AP's `√2` question — half artifact, half real, and they must be separated.** **The artifact:** `w = √2·CV`, so the `√2` in the *wobble depth* carries no information; the physical statement is **`CV = 1`.** **The real part, and AP's intuition is right:** decomposing the amplitude vector along the democratic diagonal `(1,1,1)/√3` gives `‖A_⊥‖/‖A_∥‖ = σ/μ = CV`, so **`CV` is the TANGENT of the angle to the diagonal and `CV = 1` is exactly 45°** — the spread component equals the mean component. **That is the diagonal-of-the-unit-square `√2`.** (Also **Foot 1994** in another dress — **prior art, not ours.**) **On Tsirelson: shared arena, not shared mechanism.** Both 45° bisections live in the same object in ED — the sesquilinear inner product on participation amplitudes — which is a real commonality worth exactly that much, since `√2` is the **generic Euclidean signature of a vector bisecting two orthogonal directions**. **And there is a substantive disanalogy that should stop it being pushed: Tsirelson's `2√2` is a MAXIMUM with a variational principle behind it; Koide's 45° is an INTERIOR POINT** (`CV` runs to `√2`; the quarks sit above 1). **Same arithmetic, different kind of fact.**

    **AP's 3s question — the corpus already tried, and the route was REFUTED.** **Four independent 3s, all inherited or postulated:** **spatial `D = 3`** is **P06, postulated**, with the card emphatic (*“not derived from compactification … not derived from a counting argument … not derived from anthropic reasoning”*); **three generations** not derivable (`arc-M` §6.3); **`SU(3)` colour rank** not selected; **P10's three rule-types** (matter/gauge/kernel) are a primitive classification and **not** the generation triplet, since generations are all *matter*. **And the unification route was tested rather than merely unattempted** — `substrate-evaluation/Paper_HowPDECoarseGrainReality` §2: *“a standing **wall**: the multiplicities `{1,2,3}` … are **not** selected. **ED's own route to derive them was tested and refuted (a symmetric channel-multiplet is stable for all `N`)**, so this is a genuine open limit, not an oversight.”* **So the pattern is real in the world and ED's position on it is a BANKED NEGATIVE with a stated cause. Four separate 3s, no bridge between any two — treating their coincidence as a signature would be reading a Standard-Model fact as an ED result.** `../event-density/theory/Note_MassRate_Reconciliation_And_Parametrization_2026-09-06.md`.
99. **`w(N)` ATTEMPTED 2026-09-06 and NOT COMPUTED — a banked negative, plus one restatement and one structural relocation.** #98 named this as the first real test its line admits: an ordering is the weakest thing the DOF line can produce, a value the strongest, and **the falsifier was *compute `w(N)` and check `√2` at `N = 2`*. Attempted. Not met.**

    **Method fixed first, because the corpus forbids the alternative.** `Paper_ChargeAsTopology_B4`'s retrofit trap bars bolting Koide on by matching a stray fraction, and AP's instruction was explicitly *not* to chase masses. **So the candidates were fixed from the primitives before computing, and none was adjusted after seeing its answer.**

    **What `w` IS, which is worth having on its own.** Over three 120°-separated points `Σcos = 0` and `Σcos² = 3/2` **for any `δ`**, so for the generation amplitudes `A_k = √m_k`: `μ_A = M`, `σ_A² = M²w²/2`, hence **`w = √2 · (σ_A/μ_A)`**. **`w` is `√2` times the coefficient of variation of the generation amplitudes**, and **Koide's `Q = 2/3` says exactly: the standard deviation of the three generation amplitudes EQUALS their mean, `CV = 1`.** A restatement, not a result — **but it is the variable any mechanism must deliver, and it is sharper than “why `√2`”.**

    **The three candidates, and how they fail.** **(a) Simplex sharing** (P04 non-negativity + `P-Locus-Bandwidth-Bound`'s finite total → Dirichlet(1,…,1), share ~ Beta(1, N−1)): CV `0.577, 0.707, 0.775` for `N = 2,3,4`, **approaching 1 only as `N → ∞`**. **(b) Random-phase superposition** (P11 at the limit → Rayleigh): **`0.523`, `N`-independent.** **(c) Maximum entropy** (non-negativity + fixed mean → exponential): **`CV = 1` exactly — the lepton value — but `N`-independent**, so it predicts `w = √2` for every family and **is falsified by the quarks.** **They fail in COMPLEMENTARY ways, which is the informative part: one supplies the lepton value and precludes the variation, the other supplies the variation and precludes the value. Any mechanism must do both, and neither natural route does.**

    **The one structural thing that fell out, and it is fitted from nothing.** Standard result (**inherited mathematics**): a log-concave density on `[0,∞)` has **`CV ≤ 1`, equality iff exponential.** The CVs are arithmetic from the measured `Q`: **neutrinos `≤ 0.871` (log-concave allowed), charged leptons `1.0000` (EXACTLY the boundary), down quarks `1.0922` and up quarks `1.2438` (CANNOT be log-concave).** **So Koide's `2/3` places the charged leptons exactly on the log-concavity boundary of the generation-amplitude distribution, neutrinos below and quarks above, ordered by `N`.**

    **And the caveat that matters: `CV = 1` IS Koide's relation** — the boundary statement is the same fact in another variable, **not independent evidence**. What is new is that **the boundary carries a known structural meaning**, which relocates the question **from “why `w = √2`” (a VALUE question, the half `arc-M` says ED does not do) to “why does the distribution sit at the log-concave boundary at `N = 2` and cross it as `N` grows” (a CLASSIFICATION question, the half it says ED does cleanly).** **A threshold crossing at one of four points is a coincidence until something explains it.**

    **Honest accounting, held to the same bar as a positive: this is a NEGATIVE.** The DOF line still has what it had — **an ordering**, `ν < ℓ < q`, with the neutrino direction right where the charge map was wrong — and **has NOT been upgraded to a value.** What would decide it: a distribution that is **`N`-dependent AND crosses `CV = 1` at `N = 2`**. The two natural families each supply exactly one property, **so the mechanism is not either of them and is not a small variation on either — and nothing in ED currently points at a third.** `internal notes/_check_w_of_N.py`; `../event-density/theory/Note_wN_Attempt_2026-09-06.md`.
98. **PATH B REOPENED 2026-09-06 on AP's DOF reframe — the generation wobble orders by a COMMITMENT-DOF COUNT, and the neutrino datum that falsified the corpus's charge map is the datum that supports the replacement.** AP: *“construct the pattern for other particles using your relational commitment principles, do not look for masses directly. Look for the degrees of freedom that define the commitment.”*

    **The reframe is not a workaround — it aims at the half `arc-M` says works.** `arc-M/mass_ratio_constraints` §6.3 rules Koide-style relations not derivable, **with a reason**: ED *“produces classifications and dichotomies cleanly, but does not produce continuous numerical relationships.”* **A DOF count is a classification.** So the instruction targets the working half and deliberately avoids the other; **the verdict is taken at its word, not challenged.**

    **And it diagnoses why the corpus's own map failed.** The existing reframe collapsed Koide to **`Q = 1/3 + w²/6`** (so `Q` depends **only** on the wobble depth `w`; the leptons' `2/3` is exactly `w = √2`) and then mapped `w` against **electric charge** — `w ≈ 1.61 + 0.20·Q_charge` — predicting `Q_ν ≈ 0.77`. **Falsified 2026-08:** measured splittings bound `Q_ν ≤ 0.586` regardless of the lightest mass. **It failed because charge is not a count** — fitting a line through a continuous label is precisely what arc-M rules out.

    **The new structural step, available only since Branch 3.** Canonical P11 collapses multi-channel participation to single-channel *“with the un-selected channels' phase content randomized”*, and Branch 3 defines the **Environmental** class as exactly the phase-randomized channels. **So: a commitment selects one channel and moves every other participating channel into the Environmental class** — P11 restated in band language, following from #93's licensing rather than from a new assumption. **The commitment DOF are therefore the participating non-Environmental channels, and their count is the channel multiplicity `N`.**

    **What makes that non-trivial rather than numerology:** `Paper_MS-I` §3 (absorbed by `MS-II`) derives **the gauge group `SU(N)` from channel multiplicity.** **So the same `N` is both the number of alternatives a commitment selects among (P11) and the rank of the gauge structure (MS-I/II)** — **not a new variable introduced to fit the pattern, but one ED already uses for something else.**

    **The test, which fits nothing and checks an ordering** (`internal notes/_check_commitment_dof_ordering.py`): counting distinct gauge sectors, i.e. classes of P05-transporting channel — **ν = 1** (weak), **ℓ = 2** (weak+EM), **q = 3** (weak+EM+colour) — against `w = √(6Q−2)`: **`ν ≤ 1.231 < ℓ = 1.4142 < d = 1.5447 < u = 1.7590`. Monotone, no inversion.** **And the neutrino direction is the point: the charge map sent neutrinos UP and was falsified; a count sends them DOWN, because they couple to fewer sectors — which is the direction the measurement went.**

    **What it does NOT do, stated plainly.** **(1)** It does **not split `d` from `u`** — both are 3-sector and their `w` differs by 14%, **so charge carries real information the count does not**, and a complete account needs both; this supplies one of two. **(2)** It produces **no value** — not `w = √2`, not `δ ≈ 2/9`, not any mass. **(3)** It does **not explain the `Z₃`** — checked and negative: P10's three rule-types are matter/gauge/kernel and generations are all *matter*, so that trichotomy is the wrong one; P09's `U(1)` admits `Z_n` for every `n` and picks none. **(4)** Four families, three distinct DOF values, two `w`s scheme-dependent — **weak evidence, stated as weak.**

    **Where a derivation would live, and the falsifier.** A mechanism giving **`w(N)`** — the directional intuition is that more alternatives per commitment means more phase-randomization into the Environmental class hence more spread among surviving amplitudes, **but that is an intuition, not a calculation.** Computing `w(N)` and checking it against `√2` at `N = 2` would be a **value**, and is the first real test this line admits. **Falsifier: a family whose `w` inverts against its sector count** — sharpest available is `Q_ν` measured above the charged-lepton `2/3`; current bounds (`≤ 0.586` normal, `≤ 0.500` inverted) sit well below, so the test is live and passing. `../event-density/theory/Note_CommitmentDOF_GenerationPattern_2026-09-06.md`.
97. **Relational-bands sweep run 2026-09-06 — the debt was ALREADY PAID in the source concept, and #93's “the bands are relational” is over-broad: two of the four are intrinsic.** I flagged this as a debt *created* by Branch 3 — that every downstream use of “the Adjacency band” now carries an implicit *with respect to which `S`?* while only the transport arc and the card said so. **The sweep found the opposite.**

    **`concepts/participation_bandwidth.md` already writes all four bands with explicit arguments:** **`b_int(C)`** (*bandwidth along C's own update-rule participation*), **`b_adj(C, N)`** (*with adjacency-neighbourhood `N`*), **`b_env(C, E)`** (*with environment `E`*), **`b_com(C)`**. **The relational reading is not a constraint Branch 3 imposed — it is what the source always said, and what the downstream papers dropped in transmission.** **Third instance of that exact pattern this session**, after the `w(e)` scheme translation (#87) and the “Primitive 10” header fossil. **So the debt was not created 2026-09-05; it was created whenever `b_adj(C, N)` was first written as `b_adj`.**

    **And sorting the arguments narrows the scope sharply.** **`b_int(C)` and `b_com(C)` are functions of the chain alone — CUT-INDEPENDENT.** No choice of environment moves them. **The free parameter lives at exactly one place: the Adjacency/Environmental boundary**, because `N` and `E` are complementary halves of one cut and #84 showed **a P05-transporting channel counts as Adjacency or Environmental according to that cut and nothing else.** **So #93 is right about the *partition* and over-broad about the *bands*.**

    **What that CLEARS, with no propagation needed:** **`Paper_GR-I` §2's `P-Commitment-Linear`** (`Γ_commit ∼ b_int/reserve`) uses `b_int(C)` and `b_com(C)` — **both cut-independent, so the lapse derivation and its falsifier are untouched**; **`Paper_GR-III` preamble 5's band-accounting premise** (*“the metric band and the commitment-rate-numerator band are the same”*) involves the same pair, so **it acquires no free parameter and remains exactly what it already declared**; **`Paper_GR-IV`'s commitment-reserve sector** — `b_com`, cut-independent. **The caveats I was about to spray across the gravity arc would have been wrong.**

    **What it LEAVES is one identification, not a sweep.** `Paper_UnifiedP04TransportBudget` writes `b_adj^max` per cell with **no `N`**. Restoring it: **the arc is using `N` = the cell's graph neighbourhood, i.e. `S` = the single cell** — which **conditionally answers Path C's open item 5** (*is the transport cell the individuated complex?*): **the arc is committed to `S` = the cell.** **What stays open is whether a single cell can be individuated at all**, which is the sharper version of the question.

    **A sixth naming collision found on the way, recorded not resolved.** **`b_int` means two different things:** individuation's **`b_int(S)`** is *the sum of `w(e)` over edges with both endpoints inside `S`* — an edge sum over a region — while the **Internal band's `b_int(C)`** is *bandwidth along C's own update-rule participation*, a channel-class total. **They coincide if a chain's own-rule channels are exactly the edges internal to it, which is plausible and unargued; nothing states either way** — and **`P-Commitment-Linear` uses the second sense.** Added to `ADJACENCY_AND_BAND_DISAMBIGUATION.md`.

    **Written back:** the P04 card now carries the four signatures verbatim, so the arguments are visible canonically rather than only in `concepts/`; `Paper_GR-I` and `Paper_GR-III` carry a one-line not-exposed note so this is not re-opened; the transport arc has its `N` restored with `S` = the cell named as the identification it is. **Honest summary: I flagged a debt of the wrong shape. The real one is older, narrower, and now stated.** `foundations/Note_RelationalBands_Sweep_2026-09-06.md`. Census unchanged at 173.
96. **Propagation sector re-sourced 2026-09-06 — NEITHER of the two homes was right; it is P05's, and the correction sharpens the Galilean result rather than costing it.** #95 recorded *“the propagation sector has two homes”* as a live inconsistency: `Paper_012_7` §2.1 gave *“P04 (Bandwidth) — supplies the propagation (translation-rate) sector”*, while `Paper_012_6` housed the momentum band in `P-FourBand`. **Reading them showed both wrote “P04 propagation” — and that both were wrong in the same way.**

    **Canonical P04 is a non-negative additive scalar: it carries *amount*, not *rate*.** **Translation-rate is P05** — polarity-transport along edges. **This is the third instance tonight of one category error:** attributing structure to P04 that P04 does not carry (the four-band partition → classes licensed by P05/P07/P10/P11, #93; the per-locus bound → `P-Locus-Bandwidth-Bound`, #93; now the propagation sector → P05).

    **Both papers already contained the answer.** `Paper_012_6` §3.1 had it **parenthetically** — *“Momentum-band (P04 propagation): content distinguishing substrate cells by translation-rate **(P05 polarity-transport rate)**”* — and **`Paper_012_7`'s own §3.3 describes P05 while citing P04**: *“the rate at which substrate cells **exchange polarity content** under translation.”* **Exchanging polarity content is P05 by definition.** So the fix promotes `Paper_012_6`'s parenthetical to the sourcing and corrects `Paper_012_7` to match it, rather than the reverse.

    **And the correction makes the argument BETTER, which is the part worth keeping.** `Paper_012_7`'s result is a Galilean-Jacobian asymmetry: adjacency invariant, propagation transforming. **But a Galilean boost cannot transform a non-negative additive scalar — P04 content has no frame-dependence to lose.** *“P04 propagation content shifts: a cell propagating at rate `v` propagates at rate `v+u`”* was **not merely mis-sourced, it was not well-formed**: rates boost, magnitudes do not. **Re-sourced, the asymmetry is P03 *structure* (invariant) against P05 *rate* (transforming) — a contrast between two different KINDS of object**, which is a cleaner statement of why one boosts and the other cannot, and it is the same kind-distinction the disambiguation note (#95) was written to make. **The factor-of-½ result and every audit row below step 5 are unchanged.**

    **Propagated to all eight sites across the two papers** — `Paper_012_7` §2.1, §3.2 (the transforming bullet and the asymmetry sentence), §3.3, the abstract, the claim line, the momentum-eigenvalue line, the Jacobian-input line, and **audit rows 2 and 5**; `Paper_012_6` §2.3's `P-FourBand`, §3.1 item 2, the orthogonality sentence and §4's commutation line. **A grep confirms no live “P04 propagation” attribution remains** — the four survivors are the dated correction banners quoting the old wording. `primitives/ADJACENCY_AND_BAND_DISAMBIGUATION.md` §5.1 marked **RESOLVED**; census unchanged at 173.
95. **Vocabulary disambiguation written 2026-09-05 (late) — `primitives/ADJACENCY_AND_BAND_DISAMBIGUATION.md`, the systemic fix for four collisions found in one session.** #91, #92 and #94 each flagged a naming collision **where it was found**. That is four local patches to one systemic problem, and **flagging in place is exactly what let four accumulate before anyone saw the pattern** — so this is the glossary that catches the fifth.

    **The finding that makes it worth having: “adjacency” names four different KINDS OF OBJECT, not four shades of one meaning.** **(A)** graph adjacency — the participation graph's edge **structure** (P03); **(B)** the Adjacency band — a **set of channels**, those transporting under P05 (P04 card, as amended by Branch 3); **(C)** adjacency content — a **content sector**, the position-locus one, opposed to *propagation* (`Paper_012_7`); **(D)** `∇_adj` — an **operator**, the adjoint gradient in `a_C = −∇_adj Σ_C` (P12). **They are related without being interchangeable: A is the graph, B is a class of channels defined by transporting along it, C is A's content under boost, D is a derivative operator on it.** **That is why the collisions were hard to see — substituting one for another produces a grammatical, plausible sentence.**

    **And “band” names three partitions:** **(1)** Internal / Adjacency / Environmental / Commitment-reserve, by participation-partner type (`P-Band-Partition`); **(2)** position / momentum / time / energy, by conjugate pair (**`P-FourBand`**, a separate censused postulate); **(3)** the four **Bell-test settings**, which are correlation bands and **not bandwidth at all**. **Partitions 1 and 2 are both four-fold, both partition channels, and neither contains the other.**

    **A fifth usage found while writing it, and recorded:** the **P12 card** defines `Grad` as *“the slope of event density across participation-adjacency neighborhoods”* — sense **A**, the same domain as `∇_adj`. Consistent, but it means **P12 carries two adjacency-flavoured objects**, and neither is a band. Flagged on the card, since that card is where the dwell error started.

    **The doc also gives writing rules rather than only a taxonomy** — prefer *“the Adjacency **class**”* over *“band”* (Branch 3 established these are classes of $\mathcal{K}(u)$, and “band” is what made them look like a decomposition of `b_K`); say *“position content”* rather than *“adjacency content”*; always write *“P12's adjoint gradient `∇_adj`”* with the primitive attached; always write **`P-FourBand`** rather than *“the four bands”*; and **`b_K` is bandwidth, which is not a band.** **Two live inconsistencies recorded rather than resolved:** the propagation sector has two homes (`Paper_012_7`'s *“P04 propagation content”* vs `Paper_012_6`'s `P-FourBand`), and P12's two adjacency objects. **Pointers wired into `primitives/README.md`, the P03, P04 and P12 cards** — the entry points a reader actually hits. Precedent: `../event-density/theory/d_variable_disambiguation.md` did this for the two `D` variables.
94. **The dwell arc's SECOND repair done 2026-09-05 (late) — the `∇_adj` borrow is a naming collision, its conclusion is CONTRADICTED by an existing paper, and removing it does not rescue the argument.** Branch 3 (#93) answered the July retraction's **first** defect — the argument *“hung on P04 carrying a four-band partition … not in the canonical primitive”* — on its own terms: the bands classify $\mathcal{K}(u)$, are licensed by P05/P07/P10/P11, and are merely summed by P04, **so the Internal class exists and a commitment has somewhere to collapse into.** This closes the second, which Branch 3 explicitly did not touch.

    **It is a naming collision — the fourth of the session.** **P12's `∇_adj` is an adjoint gradient: a derivative operator on the adjacency graph. The Adjacency band is a class of channels.** They share the letters and nothing else. The slide was: `∇_adj Σ_C` yields **acceleration**, so `∇_adj` is *about movement*, so **the Adjacency band is the movement band**, so a commitment landing outside it is a chain that does not move — a dwell. **Every step after the first is the collision doing the work:** an operator that differentiates *along* the adjacency graph says nothing about which *channels* sit in an Adjacency class. Joins #91's “adjacency-bandwidth” and #92's `P-FourBand` / Bell-settings.

    **And the conclusion is CONTRADICTED, not merely unsourced — which is what changes the verdict.** `qm-kinematics/Paper_012_7_AdjacencyBandwidth_Galilean`: *“P03 spatial-homogeneity forces **adjacency content (position-locus) to be boost-invariant** while **P04 propagation content (translation-rate) transforms**”*, and flatly *“the position-content does not shift kinetically.”* **In the dominant qm-kinematics usage adjacency is the POSITION sector — the boost-invariant, non-kinetic one. The dwell argument read it as the MOVEMENT sector. That is backwards.** The borrow was not an unlicensed shortcut to a right answer; **it produced a reading an existing paper denies.**

    **What the argument needed lives in a different partition.** The motion-carrying sector is **propagation / momentum**, whose band belongs to **`Paper_012_6`'s `P-FourBand`** (position/momentum/time/energy) — **sense 2** of #92, a separate named censused postulate — **not** the card's Internal / Adjacency / Environmental / Commitment-reserve partition. **So the argument reached into partition 1 for a band that lives in partition 2 and glued them with an operator from P12. A three-way collision** — and the reason it looked plausible for months is that **every piece is real ED content, just not the pieces the argument needed.**

    **And even with the right band, P11 is where it stops.** Canonical P11 collapses *“multi-channel participation to single-channel participation, with the un-selected channels' phase content randomized”* — **it says nothing about locus.** Commitment selects `K` in the four-tuple `(C,K,u,t)`; **nothing canonical says that selecting one `K` rather than another changes `u`.** So *commitment ⇒ advancement* is an identification, stated as the candidate **`P-Commitment-Advancement`** (*a commitment selecting a propagation-carrying channel advances the chain's locus; one selecting a non-propagating channel does not*) — **written into the note and deliberately NOT into a paper, so it is not adopted and does not increment the census (173, unchanged).** The July retraction asked for exactly this discipline: *“it must be proposed honestly as a new candidate primitive refinement … not asserted as something the existing 13 primitives already admit.”*

    **Verdict: repaired as a diagnosis, not as a rescue — and the route is better off.** **The dwell route is no longer blocked by a sourcing error; it is blocked by an honestly-stated missing postulate.** The earlier state was a broken argument wearing the appearance of a derivation; this one is a clearly-named gap. **The mass sector is unaffected** — `FourBand_Dependency_Check.md` established independently that `σ_τ`, Theorems M1/M2, the MR-P/MR-R massless slots and `Paper_113`'s mass-as-bandwidth-budget all stand on canonical single-scalar P04, and nothing here moves them. **Flagged in passing:** `Paper_012_7` attributes the propagation sector to *“P04 propagation content”* while `Paper_012_6` houses the momentum band in `P-FourBand` — two homes for one sector, not chased. `foundations/Note_Dwell_SecondRepair_2026-09-05.md`; follow-up banner added to `../event-density/theory/Higgs_Emergence/Dwell_Question_Answer.md`.
93. **FOUR-BAND QUESTION CLOSED 2026-09-05 (late) — AP adopted BRANCH 3. The bands are not *in* P04; they are a classification of $\mathcal{K}(u)$, *summed by* P04. `Paper_087` needed no change.** Closes #56, #57, #82–#85 and the two-day flip. **The disagreement was about location, not existence**, and both documents were mostly right: canonical `Paper_087` §P04 is right that P04 is the scalar plus additivity and nothing more; the card was right that a four-band structure exists in ED. **The card's error was one preposition.**

    **Each class has a licensing primitive, and none of them is P04.** **P07** makes channels *“structurally distinguishable carriers with intrinsic identities”*, so a classification of $\mathcal{K}$ is a **substrate fact rather than bookkeeping**; **P10** makes rule-type *“a primitive label”*, so classifying by it **reads a label the substrate already carries**; **P05** picks out the transporting channels (Adjacency); **P11** picks out the phase-randomized ones and the commitment budget (Environmental, Commitment-reserve); the remainder is Internal. **The band totals then come free from P04's additivity, which is stated over *disjoint sub-channels* — exactly what a class partition of $\mathcal{K}(u)$ supplies.**
