The sharpness half of the falsifier is only as sharp as an assumption the arc lists as open. *(The architecture-independence half is unaffected and remains the cleaner discriminator — it needs two platforms at comparable mass, which do not yet exist.)* **Recommended, small.** One clause in `Paper_056`'s update note: that the shape, like the number, is conditional on the power-law `Γ_commit` form (`Paper_054` §3.2, A→regime; arc ledger, open row). And the README's falsifier row should attach the same condition to its *sharpness* clause while leaving *architecture-independence* unconditioned. **No tier changed** — every tier involved is already correct; this is about what the retreat discloses. **What this says about the audit programme (F2).** Eight chains audited today across six arcs. This is the **first where prior work had already found what I would have found**, and the ledger shows the same moves — a spot-check, a dated staleness fix, a README de-optimisation, honest A→regime labels. **So the drift is not uniform across the corpus: it is worst where an arc has not been re-read since drafting, and effectively absent where one has.** That is a better model of the defect than “claim-strength drift is corpus-wide”, and it makes the checklist items preventive rather than merely diagnostic.
24. **F2 audit of `Paper_038_5` (Λ, `w = −1`): the paper is honest, the README is not, and the elimination leaves an interval. OPEN, 2026-09-04.** First cosmology-arc audit; chosen because *“forced”* is an item-20(c) trigger and because `w = −1` is a Tier-1 falsifier in the public README. **The paper itself is the most honest text audited today, and the drift is downstream of it.** `Paper_038_5` §3.5 is titled *“What is OPEN”*. It says `w = -1` is *“forced by **viability**”* — not by the substrate — names the **residual open** (*“whether the substrate cleanly forces the frozen-floor value or merely stipulates it”*), identifies the bootstrap knot (acceleration is needed to reach the de Sitter state that freezes `H`), and states its falsifier. Nothing in that subsection overclaims. **The public README does.** Its falsifier row reads *“Dark energy with `w ≠ −1`, evolving, or phantom — `w = −1` is **forced**”*. The paper says forced-by-viability with an open residual on whether the substrate forces or stipulates the frozen value. **If it is stipulated, `w = −1` is a commitment rather than a derivation** — still a legitimate thing to be falsified on, but not what the README's one word claims. **And the elimination is not exhaustive — this part is new and the paper does not discuss it.** §3.5 argues: a `Λ` tied to the *instantaneous* Hubble radius gives `ρ_Λ ∝ H² ⇒ w = 0`, no acceleration; therefore `Λ` is the frozen floor and `w = −1`. That is a dichotomy between two endpoints of a continuum. Writing `ρ_Λ ∝ H^{2n}`, matter domination gives `ρ_Λ ∝ a^{-3n}` and hence **`w = −1 + n`**: `n = 1` is the refuted live-tracking case, `n = 0` is the frozen floor. **The argument eliminates `n = 1` and asserts `n = 0`; it says nothing about the interval between.** Current bounds (`w = −1.03 ± 0.03`) give `|n| ≲ 0.03`, so the whole observationally allowed range sits inside the interval the argument does not address. A measured `w = −0.98` would be `n = 0.02` — not the refuted case, and not what ED claims. **This is the third instance today of the same argument shape:** decisive against one endpoint, asserting the other, with the interval carried by an unstated genericity. `GR-III` §4 kills `β = 1` and needs `β = 0` exactly (Staleness #12); `GR-IV` states `α₂ = 0` exactly on a leading-order input (#20); this states `w = −1` exactly on a two-endpoint elimination. **In all three the corpus's own source is honest and the summary is not.** **APPLIED 2026-09-04.** F3 is sharpened to *any* detected evolution at any precision, stated as a **running test** rather than a threshold, with the honest asymmetry recorded (the commitment is exact; the argument eliminates only the `n = 1` endpoint). The README row is rewritten to match and names the paper's residual. §3.5 carries the interval note. **The endpoint-elimination pattern is added to the checklist as item 20(d)**, with all three instances tabulated — it is the one part of failure mode 7 the strength-word grep cannot catch. *Originally recorded as:* **Recommended, not applied.** (i) The README row should read *“`w` measurably different from `−1`”* with the paper's residual named, not *“`w = −1` is forced”*. (ii) §3.5 should record the intermediate-tracking gap explicitly — one sentence noting that the elimination covers `n = 1` and the claim is `n = 0`, with the interval open — since it is currently invisible and it is where the data actually lives. (iii) The genuinely sharp falsifier is worth stating in its place: **ED is committed to `n = 0` exactly, so any detected evolution at any level refutes it** — which is a *stronger* and more honest position than the vague *“w ≠ −1”*, because it makes precision improvements in `w` a live test rather than a threshold to clear. *No tier changed; §3.5's own residual already carries the correct tier.*
23. **F2 audit of `Paper_066` (no-signaling): the three “independent locks” are one commitment at two levels plus an inherited bridge. OPEN, 2026-09-04.** Chosen because *“over-determined by three independent structural locks”* is exactly what checklist item 20(c) flags, and because GR-IV had just shown two *“independent”* facts to be one equation. **The three locks are not independent: they are one commitment at two levels, plus the bridge between them.** **L1** is microcausality in the emergent operator algebra — `[A_A, A_B] = 0` at spacelike separation, supplied by P-Bipartite-Mapping. **L2** is causality in the substrate — `K_V5 = 0` for spacelike-separated arguments, supplied by P-Substrate-Causality. **These are the same physical commitment stated at two description levels.** **L3** is not a third support at all: it says DCGT coarse-graining *preserves* algebra-locality, i.e. that the two levels correspond. It is the **bridge**, and without it L1 and L2 are statements about disconnected things. **So §3.4's “refutation of any single lock leaves the others intact” does not hold for L3.** Refute the bridge and L1 concerns an emergent algebra with no established relation to the substrate, while L2 concerns a substrate with no established relation to the physics — neither establishes no-signaling *for the theory*. The three do not fail independently; two of them stop meaning anything without the third. **And §3.4 concedes the L2 case in its own hedge.** It argues that refuting L2 leaves L1 intact because *“the marginal would still be independent **at the formal level**”*. That qualifier is the whole issue: formal marginal independence together with superluminal substrate signalling does not mean no-signaling holds — it means the formalism has stopped being faithful to the substrate. A lock that survives only formally is not a lock. **The load-bearing element is the one that is not ED's.** Audit row 9 tiers L3 as **I**, inherited from `Paper_073`. So the bridge on which the composition depends is inherited, while the two things it bridges are the same commitment twice. That is the opposite of over-determination: it is a single support with an inherited joint. **What is not in question.** No-signaling itself holds in ED, and this audit does not touch it — L1 is the standard argument correctly applied and is honestly tiered **D-via-I**. What fails is the *over-determination rhetoric*: §1's *“FORCED over-determined … three independent structural locks”* and §2's *“refutation of any single lock leaves the others intact”*. **Audit row 10 already tiers the composite verdict as A→position**, so the table is right and the prose is a notch above it — the same claim-strength drift recorded as checklist §10 failure mode 7, and the second instance today (after GR-IV's `α₂`/`c_s`) where two *“independent”* facts are one fact twice. **Item 20(c) flags it on the word “independent” alone.** **Recommended, not applied.** §1 and §2 should say *two levels of one causality commitment, joined by an inherited coarse-graining bridge* rather than *three independent locks*; §3.4's independence argument should be replaced by the honest structure (L3 is necessary for L1 and L2 to compose); and the genuine robustness claim that survives is worth keeping in its correct form — **the commitment is checkable at two levels, so a failure at either would be visible**, which is real and is not over-determination. *No tier changed: row 10 is already A→position.*
22. **F2 audit of `Paper_065` (monogamy) — §3.3's CKW inequality does not follow from §§3.1–3.2. OPEN, 2026-09-04.** Chosen because the entanglement arc is nominally *closed*, which is where drift survives, and because this ledger already records that *“CLOSED, no open frontier” is an overstatement*. **(a) §3.3 does not follow from §§3.1–3.2.** The chain gives `Σᵢ ℰ_{A,Bᵢ} ≤ Σᵢ W_{A,Bᵢ} = W_A ≤ W_max` — a bound by a **chain-local constant**. The displayed CKW form is `Σᵢ ℰ_{A,Bᵢ} ≤ ℰ_{A,(B₁∪…∪Bₙ)}`, a bound by a **state-dependent quantity**. These are different statements, and the second is strictly stronger whenever `ℰ_{A,union} < W_max`. Concretely: `ℰ_{A,B₁} = ℰ_{A,B₂} = 0.3 W_max` with `ℰ_{A,union} = 0.1 W_max` satisfies the derived bound (`0.6 ≤ 1`) and violates CKW (`0.6 > 0.1`). Nothing in §§3.1–3.2 relates `ℰ_{A,union}` to `W_A`, so nothing excludes it. **(b) The gap is in the stated postulate, and closing it costs the inequality.** The step works if `ℰ_{A,union} = W_A` — saturation holding **generally**, for every pair *and* for the union. But **P-Measure-Saturation is stated only for the maximally-entangled limit** (*“saturates the V5 budget in the maximally-entangled limit”*). Strengthen it to general saturation and §3.3 follows — but then `Σᵢ ℰᵢ = ℰ_{A,union}` is an **equality**, and CKW's physical content is the *strict* inequality: the deficit is the residual three-tangle, the genuinely tripartite entanglement. **A pure budget-additivity account gives equality and leaves the three-tangle nowhere to live.** The strict inequality needs the measure to be *sub-additive* relative to the budget, and nothing here supplies that. **What is actually derived, and it is worth keeping.** `Σᵢ ℰ_{A,Bᵢ} ≤ W_max`: a finite shared budget capping total pairwise entanglement, with `ℰ_{A,B₁} = W_max ⇒ ℰ_{A,Bᵢ} = 0` for `i ≥ 2` (§3.4, maximal exclusivity — which **does** follow). That is genuinely monogamy-flavoured, it is the right *kind* of mechanism, and it is a real result. **It is not CKW.** **The paper half-knows this.** §4's FORM-FORCED line reads *“Existence of finite budget partitioning”* — which is exactly the defensible claim, and matches (c). It is **§3.3** that attaches *“Form FORCED”* to the displayed CKW inequality, and the arc ledger's row inherits that: *“Monogamy (CKW-form inequality) — form D (form-forced)”*. So §3.3 and §4 disagree about what is forced, and the stronger of the two propagated. **Textbook claim-strength drift (checklist §10 failure mode 7), and checklist item 20(a) would have caught it:** the claim is stated above the tier of its weakest input, which is a postulate scoped to one limit. **Recommended, not applied.** §3.3 should state `Σᵢ ℰᵢ ≤ W_max` as the derived result and present CKW as *consistent with* it rather than forced by it; the ledger row should re-tier from **form D** to **Grounded** with CKW marked as the inherited target; and if the CKW form is wanted as a result, the open item is a **sub-additivity argument for `ℰ` against the V5 budget** — which is where the three-tangle would have to come from, and is a real piece of physics rather than an editorial fix. *No tier changed pending the author's call, since this narrows a published claim in a nominally closed arc.*
21. **F2 audit of the ℂ-selection chain (`Paper_QuantumLogicKeystone_GleasonReconstruction` §5, `Paper_001`) — first audit outside gravity, and the pattern holds. OPEN, 2026-09-04.** Chosen deliberately outside the gravity arc to test whether *“correct result, claim a notch too strong”* is a gravity habit or corpus-wide. **It is corpus-wide.** **The ℝ-exclusion's ED-side premise is a representational choice, and unlike its neighbour it is not flagged.**

§5 rules out ℝ well: it explicitly rejects the naive argument (*"a real space cannot represent a phase"* — false, real-Hilbert QM represents `U(1)` via `J` with `J² = −I`) and identifies the correct discriminator as **scalar versus operator**. A real division ring has no central scalar square root of `−1`, so if ED's phase is a *scalar* amplitude factor the field must contain `i`. That clarification is genuine and non-obvious.

The question is where *"ED's phase is a scalar"* comes from. The section cites `Paper_001`'s carrier `P_K = √b_K·e^{iπ_K}`. But **P09 supplies an angle, not a complex number** — `U(1)` is definable as `ℝ/2πℤ` or `SO(2)` with no reference to ℂ — and writing that angle as `e^{iπ}` rather than as a rotation `J(π)` on a real 2-space is a **representational choice made in `Paper_001`**. So the chain is: P09 gives an angle → `Paper_001` represents it as a complex scalar → §5 rules out ℝ because the phase is a scalar. **The step that excludes ℝ is the representation, not the primitive.**

**Both papers are honest about tier, and that is why this is a disclosure finding rather than an error.** `Paper_QuantumLogicKeystone` preamble item 2 calls it *"an account-tier selection, not a from-scratch derivation."* `Paper_001` preamble item 2 says the complex carrier is not forced from P01–P04 alone and needs P09. Neither claims a derivation. What is missing is narrower: **§5's ℝ-argument does not name its premise, while §5's ℍ-argument does** — the latter states outright that *"the ED-side premise (that ED has a genuine ⊗) is supplied by P-Bipartite-Mapping / Paper_063, a paper-specific postulate, not one of the canonical 13."* Asymmetric disclosure inside one section, and the undisclosed side is the one carrying more weight. **This answers the question left open in `Paper_PrimitiveMinimality_IndependentReconstructionProbe` §6.** Both blind runs concluded that phase and complex amplitudes must be *injected*, which raised the question of how much the Solèr selection does given P09 already carries a `U(1)`. **The answer is that the two halves differ.** The ℍ-exclusion does real work and is honestly conditioned. The ℝ-exclusion does less than it appears: **P09 alone is consistent with ℝ**, and what rules ℝ out is `Paper_001`'s scalar representation. The blind runs were right that something must be put in; ED's answer is P09 *plus* a representational commitment, and only the first is a declared primitive. **The repair is available and would strengthen the claim rather than weaken it.** Argue from the primitives why the phase must act as a **scalar** — uniformly on all amplitudes at a locus — rather than as an operator that could act channel-dependently. P03 (spatial homogeneity / no preferred channel) and P07 (channel distinguishability) are the natural candidates: if the phase were an operator it could mix channels, which P07's intrinsic-identity commitment arguably forbids. **ATTEMPTED 2026-09-04, and the proposed route fails while a better one succeeds.** P03/P07 do **not** deliver it: real-Hilbert `J` acts *within* each channel's amplitude space and never mixes channels, so P07 is not engaged, and P03 only requires `J` be locus-independent. **The working argument is continuity of observable phase:** P09 gives a continuous `U(1)` phase; `QuadraticStrain` §3.2 and `Paper_005.5` §3.3 make relative phase observable *continuously* via `cosΘ`; real amplitudes give a cross-term whose only freedom is a sign, not a continuous `cosΘ`; continuous phase dependence requires a rotation action on a 2-real-dimensional amplitude space, i.e. a complex structure; and a `J` commuting with all observables is ℂ (Stueckelberg). **This keeps the tier at account** (it is conditional on the interference results, not on the thirteen alone) **but removes the circularity** — the ℝ-exclusion now rests on ED's phase being continuously observable rather than on how `Paper_001` writes its carrier, and it is empirically anchored: sign-only interference would readmit ℝ. **APPLIED 2026-09-04.** §5's ℝ-bullet is rewritten around the four continuity steps, replacing the appeal to `Paper_001`'s carrier form; it now names its own premise (step 2, ED's interference is continuous in the relative phase, resting on P-Quadratic-Strain and the Born-rule reading), matching the ℍ-bullet. The old argument and both attempted repairs are preserved as a provenance note beneath the list, including the P03/P07 negative. Preamble item 2 now names both premises. **Tier unchanged: account.** The asymmetry is gone and the circularity with it. **No tier changed** — both papers already sit at account tier and neither claims a derivation. *Filed to the gravity ledger for continuity of the day's audit trail; the substantive home is `qm-kinematics/` and `substrate-evaluation/`, where it is cross-filed.*
20. **F2 audit of `Paper_GR-IV`: the `α₂ = 0` algebra is exact, the claim is one tier too strong, and its own source says so. OPEN, 2026-09-04.** Audited GR-IV §3 as the next un-audited load-bearing chain — the preferred-frame kill-switch, a Tier-1 falsifier in the public README, and the flagship example of the untunability reading. **The algebra checks, and is tighter than the paper states.** At `β = 0` the khronometric `α₂` numerator is `−λ + α + 2αλ`, and the scalar-luminal condition is `α = λ/(1+2λ)`. Rearranged, that condition **is** `α + 2αλ − λ = 0` — the same expression. So `α₂ = 0` and `c_s = c` are not two facts, they are one equation in two variable sets. GR-IV says the numerator *"vanishes precisely on the scalar-luminal surface"*, which is correct but understates: it is that surface. **And that is exactly why the claim is over-tiered.** `α₂ = 0` is precisely as exact as `c_s = c`, with no slack. `Paper_GR-III` preamble item 7 gives `c_s = c` as **derived at leading order**, and states outright: *“Integrating out the dissipative reserve **could renormalize the scalar speed by a small amount**; it cannot restore a second cone.”* If `c_s = c(1 + ε′)` then `α₂ = O(ε′)`, not zero. **`Paper_GR-IV` contains no instance of “leading order”, “sub-leading” or “renormaliz”** — it does not carry the caveat from its own source, and states `α₂ = 0` as *“not merely suppressed but exactly zero”*. **Three consequences, and one of them is good news.** *(i)* The public README's Tier-1 falsifier *“Any nonzero preferred-frame `α₂`”* is too strong; the honest form is *“`α₂` above the sub-leading reserve-renormalization scale”*, and **that scale is uncomputed**. *(ii)* The *“one causal-cone fact seen twice”* untunability example is weakened at the seam: `c_T = c` is structural and exact (one substrate, one transport), but `α₂ = 0` rides `c_s = c`, which is leading-order. The pairing is still one fact twice — more literally than intended, per the algebra above — but it is not two exact facts. *(iii)* **The upside:** if `α₂` is not identically zero but `O(ε′)`, and `ε′` is plausibly of the same order as `λ_local ~ ρ_event/ρ_Planck ~ 10⁻⁹³` that suppresses `α₁`, then ED predicts a **specific tiny nonzero `α₂` where GR predicts exactly zero**. Unmeasurable in practice, but it converts `α₂` from a *survival shared with GR* into a *discriminator in principle*, which is a better structural position than the paper claims for itself. **Not asserted — `ε′` has not been computed and GR-III only calls it small.** **ALL THREE APPLIED 2026-09-04.** GR-IV's abstract now says *"to the order that `c_s = c` is established"* and names `O(ε′)`; a new **F0** replaces "any nonzero `α₂`" with *"large compared with `ε′`"*, and notes that a **small** measured `α₂` would *confirm* rather than refute, since GR gives exactly zero; a new §10.5 records **O-ε′** as an open number with the reasons it is worth computing, and states that it is the same reserve integration GR-III §6 already declines, so the two open items are one. The public README falsifier row is softened to match. *Originally recorded as:* **Recommended, none applied since they change a public claim:** carry GR-III's leading-order caveat into GR-IV §3 and its abstract; soften the README falsifier row to name the renormalization scale; and record `ε′` as an open number to compute — it is the same dissipative-reserve integration GR-III already declines to perform, so the two open items are one. **No tier changed pending that decision.** *Method note: the audit found nothing wrong with the derivation. It found that a correct result is stated at a tier its own upstream source withholds — the fifth instance today of a claim being a notch stronger than what supports it.*
19. **F2 audit of `Paper_KM-I` — the corpus holds two incompatible accounts of the MOND transition, and #15 named the wrong one as standing. 2026-09-04.** Audited KM-I as the next un-audited load-bearing chain, chosen because its hinge is *forced-given-030* and 030 changed today (#13, #16). **The finding is not in KM-I.** KM-I is careful: its hinge is labelled *D conditional on I-030*, its §4.4 says outright that *"the khronon carries Paper_030's law; it does not re-derive it,"* and the IR Cancellation Requirement is boxed as *"the construction's only delicate point"* and stress-tested in §6. **The finding is that KM-I and `Paper_030` handle the high-acceleration limit by two different and incompatible mechanisms, and neither cites the other's.** `Paper_030` §7.1 uses a **switch**: three regimes with *"P14 inactive"* above `a₀`, so the cross-term does not exist where ephemerides look. `Paper_KM-I` uses a **smooth Cassini-constrained family**: preamble item 5 (*"slow-saturating members excluded by ephemerides"*), §6.3, and §7's screening discussion, where at solar accelerations `x ~ 10⁷–10⁸` the correction is *"the saturation tail plus O(α) preferred-frame terms"* — present and small, not absent. Its falsifier sentence is explicit that **"there is no mechanism here to evade the static bounds."** One paper says the term is switched off above `a₀`; the other says it is on and constrained. That is different physics, not different wording. **This corrects Staleness #15, and the correction matters.** #15 concluded that `μ` cannot be derived because *"smooth ⇒ Cassini kills it; switched ⇒ the transition is assumed"*, and called ED's transition **a switch**. That was drawn from `Paper_030` alone. **KM-I is the standing MOND result** (this ledger's own Grounded row: *"the standing MOND result — do not reinvent"*), and it is **smooth**, with the Cassini problem handled by constraining the family rather than by switching the mechanism off. So *"ED's transition is a switch"* is false as a corpus-level statement; it is `Paper_030`'s answer, and 030 is the older non-relativistic construction. **And the two findings agree on the substance.** #15 computed that `Paper_030` §6.3's profile saturates as `x^{-1/2}` and would give `8.9×10⁻⁸ m/s²` at Saturn against a `~10⁻¹⁴` bound. KM-I's preamble item 5 already excludes exactly that class — **slow-saturating members** — by name. Independently reached, same conclusion, and KM-I got there first. **The right statement is therefore narrower and stronger than #15's:** `Paper_030` §6.3's transition profile is a slow-saturating member **excluded by the corpus's own standing criterion**, and KM-I's constrained family supersedes it. `μ` is not derived in either paper, but the reason is a constrained family with an unfixed member, not an undecidable choice between smooth and switched. **Two smaller items.** *(a)* KM-I's *"conditional on I-030"* is accurate but **under-specified as of today**: 030's deep-field law now carries P-Quadratic-Strain, the restored §5.3 regime assumption (#13) and P-Bilocal-Count (#16), and KM-I names none of them. *"Forced-given-030"* reads as if 030 were settled. One sentence in §4.4 naming the inherited conditions would fix it. *(b)* If KM-I's smooth account is standing, **target #19's horizon-competition switch is answering a question posed by 030's framing**, not KM-I's. The threshold (where the regimes divide) and the shape (how the curve rounds) are separate, and #19 supplies the first — so it remains useful, but it should not be described as supplying the P14 on/off that KM-I does not use. **#15's closing line to that effect is withdrawn.** **FIXED 2026-09-04.** `Paper_030` §7.1's Newtonian row now reads *"this construction has no content here — P14 is out of scope rather than switched off"*; §7.2 is rewritten (second pass, correcting the first) as *"Parameter-free, and scoped rather than switched"*, stating that the three regimes are a **scope decomposition** and that nothing in the substrate turns off at `a₀`; and §6.3 now flags its own profile as a slow-saturating member excluded outside the deep field, with the Saturn numbers. KM-I carries the matching resolution note. **The two papers are not two mechanisms but a limited-scope construction and its completion:** 030 supplies the deep-field law, KM-I supplies the crossover and carries the ephemeris constraint. **No tier changed in KM-I.** The audit found nothing wrong in it; what it found is a corpus-level inconsistency between two papers and an error in this ledger's own #15.
18. **Chasing the factor of 4: two channel counts, and a multiplicity bound that collides with Bekenstein — OPEN, 2026-09-04.** Ran down the `1 vs 1/4` coefficient `Paper_025` §6 reports as "order-unity" (see #17's F-P entry). **(a) `Paper_043` states its own postulate two ways, differing by `4π`.** Its **P-Horizon-Participation** reads `N = A/(4πℓ_P²)`, while §1's derivation chain cites `Paper_025`'s `N = 4πR²/ℓ_ED² = A/ℓ_ED²`, and audit row 3 records `N = A/ℓ_P²` "up to constants". Three statements, two values, one paper. **(b) The difference is load-bearing, and the branch `Paper_025` supplies is the one that fails.** With `S = N·log g` (row 6) against Bekenstein `S = A/(4ℓ²)`: under `N = A/ℓ²` the required multiplicity is `log g = 1/4`, i.e. **`g ≈ 1.28`** — which **violates `P-Multiplicity-g`'s own stated `g ≥ 2`**. At the postulate's minimum `g = 2`, ED over-produces horizon entropy by **2.77×** Bekenstein. Under `N = A/(4πℓ²)` the requirement is `log g = π`, `g ≈ 23.1`, which satisfies `g ≥ 2`. **The two internal counts give opposite verdicts on whether the corpus is consistent with Bekenstein–Hawking.** **(c) There is no dishonesty here, and that is worth saying.** `Paper_043` is titled *"Area-Law Form FORCED; Coefficient (log g) INHERITED"*, its item 1 explicitly disclaims deriving the `1/4`, and item 5 says "omitting numerical prefactors". The defect is that **"inherited" was doing more work than stated**: `log g` is not free, it is bounded below by the `g ≥ 2` postulate, and that bound collides with the matching under the corpus's own count. **(b)/(d) CORRECTED 2026-09-04, same day: both were artifacts of reading a superseded paper as current.** `Paper_HorizonTilingThreeCounts` §4 already resolves this and states it outright — *"the earlier 'coefficient inherited' reading (Paper_043) is superseded — the coefficient is derived, and it lives in the thermal route, not the tiling."* The standing account is **tiling × thermal factor**: three independent counts (holographic, dynamical frozen-state, straddling-edge) put the tiling near **one** bit per Planck cell, and the `1/4` is supplied by `Paper_BH_Thermal2Pi`. Because the counts land near 1 and not near `1/4`, **the coefficient was never in the count** — so there is no entropy over-production to suppress, the `g ≥ 2` collision is an artifact of the superseded `S = N log g` placement, and the two routes are *not* in conflict. **The real defect is narrower and is a pointer one:** `Paper_043` carried no supersession banner, so a reader arriving there met the broken framing as current. Banner added. *Method note worth keeping: this was one step from a correlated-cell suppression mechanism to fix a problem the corpus had already dissolved. The read-first check caught it, which is the whole reason the rule exists.* The original (d) text follows for the audit trail. **(d) [superseded] The sharper collision is with `Paper_BH_Thermal2Pi`.** That paper claims to *derive* `S = A/4` thermodynamically — `½` from `κ = 1/(2r_s)` and `2π` from near-horizon Rindler smoothness — with no reference to the channel count. If it stands, `log g` is **not** free to be inherited: it is determined, equals `1/4`, and is then forbidden by `g ≥ 2` under `Paper_025`'s count. **Two routes the corpus treats as independent are in conflict through the multiplicity bound, and nothing records it.** **Three ways out, none chosen here.** *(i)* `P-Horizon-Participation`'s `4π` version is the intended count and `Paper_025`'s is being misapplied at the horizon — cheapest, but it makes the two papers disagree about `N` rather than about `g`, and `Paper_025` §6 explicitly compares *its* count with Bekenstein. *(ii)* `g ≥ 2` should be `g > 1`; the postulate's floor may be an unconsidered tidiness rather than a commitment, though a cell with `g < 2` distinguishable configurations is hard to read. *(iii)* **The most promising:** audit row 6 is labelled *"standard entropy on independent cells"*, and horizon cells need not be independent. Correlated cells give `S < N log g`, which relaxes the bound and would let `g ≥ 2` coexist with `S = A/4`. That is a substantive physical claim about horizon correlations, it is exactly the kind of thing ED's V5 cross-chain structure could supply, and **it is not made anywhere.** **(a) FIXED 2026-09-04, and it went the inconvenient way.** `Paper_043`'s P-Horizon-Participation is corrected to `N = A/ℓ_P²`: `Paper_025` derives the bound as area-over-footprint with an `ℓ²` footprint, `4πR²` **is** `A`, and two of the paper's three statements plus the upstream derivation already agreed. The stray `4π` is what a `4πR² → A` substitution looks like applied twice. **This closes escape route (i) and sharpens the problem:** the admissible branch was the erroneous one, so the requirement `log g = 1/4` against the `g ≥ 2` floor now stands unambiguously, with horizon entropy `2.77×` Bekenstein at the floor. **The arc now has a live coefficient problem where it had an ambiguity** — the more useful state. Routes (ii) and (iii) remain; (iii) is the promising one. Then state which of (ii)/(iii) carries the coefficient. **No tier changed:** `Paper_043`'s area-law *form* is unaffected, and it never claimed the coefficient. *Filed to the black-hole arc ledger as well; the substantive work is there, not in gravity.*
17. **Live audit of the `Paper_027` Newton chain — 2026-09-04. Two real findings, one false positive, and one error of mine rather than the corpus's.** Both frontier families were given the 026/027 chain cold. **(a) `G = c³ℓ_ED²/ħ` is dimensional matching, not derivation — REAL, and sharper than what is recorded.** `Paper_027` §5.3 already says the identification is "a dimensional rearrangement of the standard Planck-length definition" and tiers `ℓ_ED = ℓ_P` as empirical. The audit adds two points the paper does not make: dimensional compatibility gives `G ~ c³ℓ_ED²/ħ` and **not** `=`, since any dimensionless `C` is admissible, so the equality needs a postulate fixing `C = 1`; and `c` and `ħ` are never introduced in the postulate set at all. **The consequence for the parsimony claim (§4.7, "reduces the number of independent fundamental constants by one") is the important part:** that holds only if `ℓ_ED` is fixed **independently of `G`**. If `ℓ_ED` is fixed by matching observed gravitational strength — which is what §5.3 says — then no constant has been eliminated, only renamed. *The defence, not currently made in the paper: `ℓ_ED` is also the UV cutoff (`Paper_111`) and the horizon-count grain (`Paper_025`), so fixing it once and spending it several times is a genuine reduction. That argument should be in `Paper_027` §4.7 and is not.* **F-P RUN 2026-09-04 — it does not fire, and it cannot fire as written.** All three companion roles **inherit** `ℓ_ED` from `Paper_027` §5.3 rather than determining it: `Paper_025` labels the identification **I** (*"empirically identified with ℓ_P via Newton-recovery"*), `Paper_111` says the cutoff is *"INHERITED via Planck-scale matching"*, and `Paper_043` needs P-Horizon-Participation to fix its count. **One determination, three uses.** The defence written into §1.2 earlier the same day said "spent in several independent places", which is true of the uses and false of the determinations; **it has been corrected in place.** The surviving claim is an accounting one about the *input list* — a rival needs `G` plus a grain plus a cutoff, ED needs one scale — not a claim of predictive success. **F-P reformulated** to fire only if some role ever determines `ℓ_ED` independently of `G` and disagrees with `√(ħG/c³)`; conditional, since no independent determination exists today. **One quasi-independent comparison does exist and is off by 4:** `Paper_025` §6 finds its bound matches Bekenstein's in form *"up to the dimensionless coefficient (1 vs. 1/4)"*. That is the only outside check on the shared value, and in this arc the forms have been reliable while the coefficients have not — flagged, not dismissed. **BOTH EDITS APPLIED 2026-09-04.** `Paper_027` §2 now declares **P-G-Closure**; §5.2's "unique combination" is corrected to *unique in its powers, not its coefficient*, with a scope note recording that `κ_V1σ(M) = GM` defines the product in terms of `G` and so cannot independently fix it. The parsimony claim in §1.2 now carries the multi-use defence: `ℓ_ED` is one postulated scale spent in the horizon channel count (025), the BH area law and entropy coefficient (043, BH_Thermal2Pi), and the UV cutoff (111), so a framework carrying `G` as an independent input must separately supply a grain and a cutoff — ED supplies one number and uses it three times. The claim is now explicitly *about the count of independent inputs across the corpus, not about this derivation in isolation*, with falsifier **F-P**: if `ℓ_ED` needed a different value in any of those roles than the one reproducing `G`, the reduction is spurious. *(Section number correction: this was called §4.7 in session notes; the parsimony claim is in §1.2 and §4 ends at §4.6.)* **(b) `N(R)` does no work in the `1/R` — REAL, and it is my error, not the corpus's.** Because the count cancels *exactly*, the radial form comes wholly from V1's inherited envelope and the same cancellation runs in any spatial dimension. `Paper_027` never claims otherwise. The claim *"inverse-square is P06 showing up, not a balance of two effects"* was written by me into `Desktop/ED New Organization/What ED Is_Technical.md` on 2026-09-04 and is now corrected there. What P06/P08 actually buy is the **distribution** step `σ_ch = σ(M)/N(R)`, which is what prevents the naive `Φ ∝ R`, plus the scale that later fixes `G`. *A summary document invented a claim the papers do not make: the read-first rule exists for exactly this and I broke it.* **(c) P-Codim-1 mislabelled — FALSE POSITIVE, held to the same bar as a hit.** One audit argued that a codimension-1 object in 3-space is a 2D sheet, which meets a 2-sphere in curves rather than in `ℓ²` patches, so the count `A/ℓ²` misapplies intersection geometry. Standard usage would make that right, but `Paper_025` §2 **defines its own term**: *"when a channel crosses an n-dimensional substrate-graph surface, it occupies a region of n-dimensional extent at the substrate scale"* — a 2D footprint on a 2-surface, exactly what the count needs. Its audit table already labels the row **P** and states it is not derivable from P02+P07. **No defect.** *Worth keeping as a naming hazard: the label invites precisely this misreading, and a reader who knows differential geometry will make it.*
16. **Channel-count factors dropped in the bilocal geometric mean — ~~OPEN~~ **DISCHARGED 2026-09-04**, and this row was stale against its own paper until the status pass (#42).** `Paper_030` §4.2 now displays the cancellation explicitly and states the condition it needs as the labelled postulate **P-Bilocal-Count** (`N_bilocal = N_horizon = N(R) = 4πR²/ℓ_ED²`), with falsifier **F-BC** and an explicit statement of what breaks without it (if `N_horizon` were `N(R_H)` instead, the ratio would be `R/R_H ≈ 2.3×10⁻⁶` at 10 kpc and the deep-MOND result would be destroyed). The paper carries *“A flag recorded here on 2026-09-04 has been discharged by the rewrite below”* — **the fix landed in the paper and this row was never updated.** Reverse of the usual direction, and the more costly one: a reader choosing work sees OPEN and re-does finished work. *Original finding retained below for the audit trail.* **`Paper_030` §4.2 defines two channel-count factors and drops both in the next equation.** It states `b_K^{loc}(R) ∝ GM/(R·N(R))` and `b_K^{horizon}(R) ∝ a₀R/N_horizon`, so the geometric mean of those two quantities is `√(GMa₀ / (N(R)·N_horizon))`. The equation immediately following instead reads `√(GM/R · a₀R) = √(GMa₀)`, with both count factors absent. The only bridge is the clause *"for substrate-level bandwidth conventions chosen consistent with Papers_027 and 029"*. **The magnitude is not marginal:** `N(R) = 4πR²/ℓ_P² ≈ 4.6×10¹¹¹` at 10 kpc, so the dropped `1/√N(R)` alone is `~10⁻⁵⁶`, before `N_horizon`. **`Paper_QuadraticStrain_v1` inherits the post-drop form**: its §2 lists `b^{(L)} ∝ GM/R` and §5.1 uses it to obtain `√(GMa₀)` — while its own §4 uses `b_K^{(L)} ∝ GM/(R·N(R))` for the same symbol, so the two forms sit in one paper, differing by `N(R)`. **This is the third instance of one defect class, and it is the same class as Staleness #10 and #13:** an unfixed normalization absorbing whatever factor the result requires, with the absorption carried by a clause rather than a derivation. In #10 it was `N_substrate` erasing a `2/3` and supplying a `1/(2π)`; here it is "conventions chosen consistent with" erasing `N(R)·N_horizon`. **Fairness note, and it matters:** a strain-to-potential normalization may legitimately carry channel-count factors — `Paper_026`/`027` show exactly such a cancellation working correctly, where `N(R)` divides out because the source content is *distributed* across channels. The defect is not that count factors cancel; it is that here they are written into the definitions and then absent from the result with no cancellation shown, so the normalization is again doing unbounded and unaudited work. **Provenance: this was found by an outside model, not in-house.** The `Paper_QuadraticStrain_v1` chain was put to two frontier families as a live audit (`Note` §—, probe note §5d). Both located the diagonal-removal defect this ledger already had as #13; **one additionally found this, which neither the in-house read nor the item-19 symbol sweep had caught** — the sweep missed it because `b^{(L)}` and `b_K^{loc}` are different tokens and both forms are separately *stated*, so a form-collision search does not fire. **Item 19 should be strengthened: the sweep must follow a quantity across renamings, not just across occurrences of one spelling.** A second audit point, also correct and separately recorded: `R` carries two meanings inside `√(b^{(L)}b^{(H)})` — local radius in `GM/R`, and whatever makes `a₀R` a horizon quantity — and if both are the local radius then `b^{(H)}` grows without bound with distance from the local mass, which is the same incoherence #13 records from the diagonal side. **SETTLED 2026-09-04 by reading `Paper_027` §§4.3–4.4. The cancellation exists and is legitimate; the audit finding stands as a presentation defect, not a physics error; and the clause conceals one specific, decidable assumption.** *(1) The mechanism is real.* `Paper_027` §4.3 distributes the source content, `σ_ch = σ(M)/N(R)`, structurally from P03 translation-invariance plus P07 distinguishability plus the holographic count — explicitly *not* a phenomenological partition. §4.4, titled *"the channel-count factors cancel"*, then sums over `N(R)` channels and the factors divide out exactly, `Φ = −κ_V1σ(M)/R`. That section even records the round-1 error of losing the cancellation and getting `Φ ∝ R`, so the corpus knows this trap by name. *(2) It extends to the geometric mean, conditionally.* Summing the cross-term over bilocal channels gives `N_bilocal·√((b_L/N)(b_H/N_h)) = √(b_L b_H)·N_bilocal/√(N·N_h)`, so the `√(GMa₀)` of `Paper_030` §4.2 is exactly right **iff `N_bilocal = √(N·N_horizon)`** — which holds trivially when each of the `N(R)` channels through Σ_R carries both local and horizon content, the natural reading of the construction. *(3) The hidden assumption is `N_horizon = N(R)`.* `Paper_030` line 163 introduces `N_horizon` as "channels connecting chain to dipole-projected cosmic horizon" and **never assigns it a value**; it appears nowhere else in the arc. If it is instead the count on the cosmic surface, `N(R_H)`, the surviving factor is `√(N(R)/N(R_H)) = R/R_H ≈ 2.3×10⁻⁶` at 10 kpc — which would destroy the deep-MOND result. **So the clause "conventions chosen consistent with" is standing where a named identification belongs, and the identification is defensible** (horizon content reaching the chain crosses the same enclosing 2-surface) **but it is an assumption, not a derivation.** **Verdict: no tier change to `√(a_N a₀)`** — it was already conditional on P14/P-Quadratic-Strain and is now additionally conditional on one named identification. **FIX APPLIED 2026-09-04.** `Paper_030` §4.2 now displays the full sum `N_bilocal·√(b_loc·b_hor) = √(GMa₀)·N_bilocal/√(N(R)N_horizon)`, notes that the cross-term is quadratic in amplitude so each factor contributes `1/√N` rather than `1/N`, and names the missing identification as a labelled postulate **P-Bilocal-Count** with its justification (P-Codim-1 puts horizon content across the same enclosing 2-surface; P03 gives no channel a preferred source). The alternative reading and its `R/R_H` consequence are stated, with falsifier **F-BC**. Registered in the Postulated section above. **Audit credit stands:** the finding was correct about what is displayed, and the corpus is better for having the condition named rather than absorbed.
15. **μ cannot be derived as a smooth function, and `Paper_030` §6.3/§7.2 overstate what exists — 2026-09-04. ⚠ THE CLOSED FORM BELOW IS SUPERSEDED (updated 2026-09-04, #42).** The profile this item computes from, `a = a_N + a₀ + √(a_N a₀)`, **no longer exists**: ≦37 removed the standalone `a₀` at source (`Σ₀` is a content normalization, no gradient taken). The corrected profile is **`a = a_N + √(a_N a₀)`**, giving with `s = √(a_N/a₀)` the closed form **`μ = s/(s+1)`**, not `s²/(s²+s+1)`. *(Verified numerically over `a_N/a₀ = 10⁴…10⁻⁴`; the two differ substantially — at `a_N = a₀`, `μ = 0.500` against the old `0.333`.)* **Both conclusions of this item survive unchanged, which is why it is updated rather than withdrawn:** the saturation is still `1-μ = 1/(1+s) ~ x^{-1/2}`, so the Cassini exclusion outside the deep field stands; and §§6.3/7.2 were reworded accordingly. **What DID change is the diagnosis:** ≦34 re-files `μ` as the coarse-grained **V5 envelope shape**, which `Paper_090` §7.2 lists as value-layer — so `μ` is **inherited, not an open derivation**, and this item's title (*“cannot be derived”*) is right for the wrong reason. *Original text below.* Attempted the derivation of the MOND interpolation function from the substrate. **ED has a candidate and it is written down:** `Paper_030` §6.3 gives `a = a_N + a₀ + √(a_N a₀)`, which with `s = √(a_N/a₀)` is the closed form `a/a₀ = s²+s+1`, `μ = s²/(s²+s+1)`. **Its saturation is `1-μ ~ x^{-1/2}`** — far slower than the simple (`x^{-1}`) or standard (`x^{-2}`) families. The `√(a_N a₀)` term is a `1/R` force, **not** degenerate with a `GM_☉` rescaling, and it gives **8.9×10⁻⁸ m/s²** of anomalous acceleration at Saturn against a Cassini-class bound near **10⁻¹⁴** — excluded by ~7 orders. **`Paper_KM-I` preamble item 5 already states the criterion** ("a Cassini-constrained family, slow-saturating members excluded by ephemerides") **and neither paper notices that §6.3's profile is such a member.** *ED is not refuted*, because `Paper_030` §7.1 switches P14 **inactive** in the Newtonian regime. But that escape contradicts §7.2's "smooth and parameter-free crossover": the profile is **three branches with P14 toggled at the boundaries**, and the toggle is the Staleness #13 regime assumption. **§6.3's "no interpolation function" claim does not hold** — a piecewise profile with an assumed switch *is* an interpolation function, minus the smoothness. **The two are exclusive:** smooth ⇒ Cassini kills it; switched ⇒ the transition is assumed, not derived. **SCOPED 2026-09-04 by item 19: the conclusion below is `Paper_030`'s, not the corpus's.** `Paper_KM-I` — the **standing** MOND result — uses a smooth Cassini-constrained interpolation family, not a switch, and states plainly that *"there is no mechanism here to evade the static bounds."* What survives from this item is the sharper half: `Paper_030` §6.3's profile saturates as `x^{-1/2}` and is a **slow-saturating member excluded by KM-I's own stated criterion**. **Convergence with item 14:** the horizon-competition mechanism *is* that switch, and switches the right way — above `cH₀` the chain's own Rindler horizon lies inside the cosmic one, cosmic content is cut off, and the bilocal term has nothing to couple to. It would **derive §7.1's assumed regime structure**, which is what Cassini demands. Residual: the switch sits at `cH₀` not `a₀` (item 14's 5.67), and a sharp switch is not the gradual transition rotation curves show. **Recommended: §7.2's "smooth" and §6.3's "no interpolation function" should be reworded; the honest claim is a derived deep-MOND asymptote plus an assumed regime switch.** New falsifier logged on the threshold note as F4: if the cross-term is ever shown active at solar-system accelerations, `Paper_030` is refuted outright.
14. **A threshold mechanism for `a₀`, and a convergence that isolates the `2π` — 2026-09-04.** (`Note_a0_ThresholdFromHorizonCompetition.md`, opened as research target #19.) `Paper_047_5` already holds the ingredient at M3: Rindler acceleration horizons and cosmological horizons are **the same ED substrate object**, the V5-saturated boundary where `Γ_cross` collapses, differing only in which axis parameterises it. A chain therefore has **two** saturation boundaries — its own at `R_a = c²/a` and the cosmic one at `R_H = c/H₀` — and only the nearer binds. That is a **discrete switch**, and it is the same competition `Paper_028` §3.2 runs to fix `R_H` itself. The switch is at `c²/a = c/H₀`, i.e. **`a = cH₀`**: above it the chain's own horizon is inside the cosmic one (Newtonian), below it the cosmic horizon binds (deep-MOND). **This answers target #19's question without a new postulate:** the threshold is not in the response, which stays linear in `|a|` per `Paper_029`; it is in which boundary saturates. **The convergence is the important part.** This lands on `a₀ = cH₀` bare. Target #18's Route 2, from entirely different machinery (Rindler smoothness + de Sitter temperature), found the *symmetric* condition `T_a = T_H` also gives `cH₀`, with only an unmotivated asymmetric condition producing the `2π`. **Two independent mechanisms now reach `a₀ ~ cH₀`; neither produces the `2π`; and the azimuthal-Fourier derivation that claims it provably cancels (Staleness #10).** Three routes to the scale, none to the coefficient. That *strengthens* horizon-tying — the substantive claim now has two independent derivations — and makes the coefficient look **inherited**. **Numerical gap, unresolved:** `cH₀ ≈ 6.8×10⁻¹⁰` against a fitted `a₀ ≈ 1.2×10⁻¹⁰`, a factor of ~5.7 ≈ `2π`. Either the factor is real and unexplained (= target #18), or a fitted `a₀` in an interpolation function `μ(x)` is not the same object as a mechanism's crossover point. **CHECK RUN 2026-09-04 — the strong form is refuted.** In any law `μ(a/a₀)a = a_N` the asymptotes `√(a_N a₀)` and `a_N` cross at `a_N = a₀`, so the fitted `a₀` **is** the transition location for every `μ` family: the shape of `μ` rounds the corner, it does not move it (checked across three families; the solution at `a_N = a₀` lands at 1.10–1.62 `a₀`). No `μ` puts the transition at `cH₀` while `a₀` is `cH₀/(2π)`. A weak form survives — the switch might mark the *onset* of departure rather than the crossing, and the 10%-departure point ranges 1.0–9.0 `a₀` across families, bracketing 5.67 — but that range spans an order of magnitude, `μ` is fitted not derived, and picking “10% departure” after seeing the number is not evidence. **Recorded as not-refuted, not as support.** Net: 5.67 is a genuine residual (5.42–5.99 over the Hubble-tension range, i.e. `2π` to within 5–14%), and it is *the same* residual as Staleness #10's. **Every route reduces to comparing the chain's acceleration scale against `cH₀` while the measurement says `cH₀/(2π)`: one unexplained factor at the horizon-to-chain join, identical in every derivation.** Deriving `μ` from the substrate would make this a sharp test. **Pay-off if the switch can be shown rather than asserted:** `Paper_030` §5.3's regime assumption — restored under Staleness #13 — becomes *derivable*, since "the cross-term dominates in deep-MOND" is exactly "the cosmic boundary binds below `cH₀`". That would restore QuadraticStrain's second discharge by a valid route. Not claimed. **No tier changes.**
13. **QuadraticStrain — the second discharge is inconsistent. ~~OPEN~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-04** — see #44.** The dilemma below is dissolved rather than chosen between: #36's source-vs-response asymmetry removes branch (i)'s cost, so the paper sits on branch (i) with `b^(H)` isotropic and the `Σ₀` removal justified without a dipole. The `Θ_LH` repair floated at the end of this item is **closed** — §5.1 factors `cosΘ_LH` out of the channel sum, so it cannot be channel-dependent. *Original finding retained below.* Adversarial read of `Paper_QuadraticStrain_v1` §§2–6, requested as a fresh audit target after Staleness #10. **`b^(H)` is used with two incompatible meanings, and the R-linear form is load-bearing for both.** §2 inherits the per-channel convention `b^(H) ∝ a₀R` (Paper_030 §4.2) as a non-negative bandwidth — it must be, since P04 requires `b_K ≥ 0` and §5.1 takes its square root. That R-linear form is exactly what makes the geometric mean work: `√(GM/R · a₀R) = √(GMa₀)`, R-independent, which is the whole point. **The same object, on the diagonal, is `Σ₀ = a₀R`** — the term §6.2 removes as "inconsistent with Paper_029's dipole." Under P-Quadratic-Strain, `|P^L + P^H|² = b^L + b^H + 2√(b^L b^H)cosΘ` is a single algebraic identity: the second and third terms both follow from `P^H ≠ 0` on the channel. Keeping the cross-term while deleting the self-term is not a physical claim about dipoles; it is dropping one term of an expansion the paper's own postulate generates. **Both repairs cost something, which is why this needs a decision rather than a patch.** *(i) If `b^(H)` is isotropic* — which is what §2 and §5.1 actually use — the geometric mean, the P14 discharge and BTFR all stand, but §6.2's removal of `Σ₀` loses its justification, the diagonal contributes a constant `a₀` (gradient of `a₀R`), and Paper_030's regime assumption returns. The **second** discharge fails. *(ii) If `b^(H)` carries the genuine dipole structure* `∝ |a|cosθ` that §6.1–6.2 rely on, it is sign-indefinite, so it is not a bandwidth under P04 and `√(b^L b^H)` is imaginary over half the sphere. Then the **first** discharge fails too. As written the paper is on branch (i) and §6 is the part that does not fit. **Candidate repair, not attempted here.** The horizon's directional, acceleration-induced character could be carried by the relative phase `Θ_LH` rather than by `b^(H)` — a `cosθ` structure sits naturally in an angle. That keeps `b^(H)` non-negative and isotropic, preserving §5 intact. It does **not** by itself remove the diagonal, so §6.3's ratio effect would still need an independent argument for why the horizon self-term does not contribute a gradient. **Scope.** The first discharge (P14 → the geometric mean forced as the off-diagonal of `|P^L + P^H|²`) is a good argument and survives on branch (i); it is the stronger half of the paper. The MOND phenomenology, BTFR slope-4 and Newtonian recovery are unaffected on that branch. What is at risk is specifically §8's claim of **two** discharges and the "no regime assumption is needed" line in §6.3. Note also that this compounds with Staleness #10: §6.1 rests on Paper_029's `1/(2π)` dipole derivation, itself now disputed. **Sharpened by the item-19 sweep, same day: `Paper_030` pre-emptively answers the argument §6.2 uses against it, and §6.2 does not cite the answer.** `Paper_030` line 123 states outright: *"Σ₀(R) = −a₀R is **not** a separately-postulated stability-landscape function; it is the coarse-grained potential **derived from Paper_029's dipole-projection mechanism via radial integration**."* QuadraticStrain §6.2 rejects it on the ground that "there is no static, source-independent, isotropic field of strength a₀ from which a radial integration could build a potential −a₀R" — arguing against a monopole reading Paper_030 explicitly disclaims. Two papers now disagree in the record about whether the same radial integration is legitimate, and the later one does not engage the earlier one's stated justification. **The empirical constraint is the thing that actually decides this**, and `Paper_030` line 145 names it: with Σ₀ present and additive, deep-MOND gives `a ≈ a₀` constant, hence `v² = a₀R`, which BTFR data refutes. So Σ₀ must be either removed (QuadraticStrain's route) or suppressed (Paper_030's regime assumption). It cannot simply stand. That is the real pressure behind both papers' moves, and any repair has to clear it. **A third option neither paper takes:** `Paper_029` §3.3 has the horizon contribution existing *only for an accelerating chain* and proportional to its own acceleration — which makes it a **response** term, not a **source** term, and a response does not enter the potential additively at all. That would remove Σ₀ for a reason that survives §6.2's problem, and it is a research question rather than an edit. *The paper's §2.5 audit table is otherwise exemplary — eleven labelled rows, the phase residual honestly flagged A/OPEN at row 11 — and this slipped past it because the inconsistency lives **between** rows 6 and 7 rather than inside either. Same structural class as Staleness #10: a quantity carrying one meaning in one section and another meaning three sections later, invisible to a per-row check.* **RESOLVED 2026-09-04 — branch (i) taken, and the defect is upstream.** §6.2's *conclusion* stands but its *argument* does not: `Σ₀ = −a₀R` is not isotropic — a linear potential with constant gradient along the chain–mass axis is exactly what an acceleration-aligned dipole gives, and `Paper_030` §3.2 integrates along that axis — so anisotropy does not disqualify it, and §6.2 never engages `Paper_030` line 123's stated provenance. **The real defect is one word in `Paper_030` §3.2:** it integrates *"the **constant** cosmic-anisotropy acceleration a₀"*, while `Paper_029` §§3.3/5.1 make the horizon content first order in the chain's own `|a|` and **zero for a non-accelerating chain**. That step converts a **response** into a **source**. Flagged at source in `Paper_030`. **A third branch was attempted on that basis and fails:** restoring `b^{(H)} ∝ H₀|a|` would have been free — the diagonal stays, so the algebra is honest, and it vanishes as `a → 0`, so no constant-`a₀` floor and BTFR is safe — but the cross-term then gives `a² ∝ GM H₀|a|/R²`, hence `a ∝ GM H₀/R²`, a Newtonian-like `1/R²` scaling rather than `√(a_N a₀)`. **The naive restoration breaks MOND. Recorded as a negative.** **Branch taken: (i).** `b^{(H)}` isotropic and R-linear as §2/§5.1 already use it; the diagonal **stays** (it is generated by the paper's own postulate and cannot be dropped selectively); `Paper_030` §5.3's regime assumption is **restored**. QuadraticStrain audit row 7 **WITHDRAWN**, row 8 re-tiered to **P (regime assumption restored)**, §8's "two postulates to one" reads **one and a half**. §5's geometric-mean forcing and the P14 discharge are **unaffected and remain the strong half**. **What this buys is worth more than the discharge it costs:** the formal inconsistency is now a specific physical question — *`Paper_029` gives a horizon response first-order in `|a|`, and MOND requires a threshold at `a₀`; how does a linear response produce a threshold?* Neither paper asks it, it was invisible while `Σ₀` was carried as a constant source, and it is arguably the deepest open question in ED's MOND account. **Promoted to an open research item.**
12. **P-Commitment-Linear / GR-III §4 traced 2026-09-04 — sound in kind, one asymmetry worth naming. No tier change.** Audited the way `N_substrate` was audited for the a₀ `1/(2π)` (Staleness #10), and the verdict is different: **this is not that class of problem.** GR-III §4 states its premise inside the boxed claim itself (*"modulo one band-accounting premise: that the metric band and the rate-numerator band are the same `b_int`; named, not proven"*), scopes its falsifier at line 211 to the exact condition **β ≠ 0** rather than to the endpoint it argues hardest against, and states the counterfactual plainly (*"had P04 defined the reserve as a fixed fraction of the total or supplied a replenishment band, ED would have been Nordström"*). Contrast 028/029, where §5.3 claimed "no free parameter" while the choice sat unflagged in §5.1. The algebra also checks: with `Γ ∝ b^α`, the GR-I §4.2 null condition `0 = −N² + b⁻¹b^{2α}` gives `N² ~ b^{2α−1}` and `g₀₀g_rr ~ −b^{2(α−1)}`, so α = 1 is Einstein and α = 0 is Nordström, exactly as stated.

    **The asymmetry.** Writing `reserve ∼ b_int^β`, so `α = 1 − β`, the §4 argument is *decisive against β = 1*: Nordström needs the reserve **raised** where `b_int` is high, which is replenishment, which P11 forbids. But Einstein needs **β = 0 exactly**, and any intermediate `0 < β < 1` — a reserve merely correlated with ambient capacity without tracking it — gives `g₀₀g_rr ≠ −1` and no Schwarzschild relation. The step from "does not track" to "is exactly zero" is carried by the word *"generically"* and is not separately argued. The paper's own falsifier is correctly scoped to β ≠ 0, so the claim is stated at the right strength; it is the §4 body's supporting argument that is stronger at one endpoint than across the interval. **Recommended: one added sentence in §4 (or a GR-III preamble item) stating that the replenishment argument bounds β away from 1 and that β = 0 exactly is a genericity claim on the P04 band structure.** Not a re-tier: the ledger already carries this as *D-cond on a named band-accounting premise*, which remains accurate.

    **One question raised, not resolved.** If the reserve is monotone-draining with no replenishment (§4, §6), what sustains a steady `Γ ∼ b_int/reserve` in a static exterior? As the reserve drains, `Γ` grows without bound; §7 uses precisely that mechanism for horizon formation (*"where the reserve is exhausted, so the cut is frozen"*). Either the reserve is a per-chain carried budget rather than a local field — in which case "co-scaling with `b_int`" needs restating in those terms — or a static exterior needs something holding the reserve steady, which would be the replenishment §4 forbids. Likely a reading error on the auditor's part rather than a defect; flagged for whoever next opens GR-III.

11. **`N² ~ b` is the arc's least-obvious step — measured 2026-09-04, record-only.** In an independent-reconstruction probe (`../substrate-evaluation/Paper_PrimitiveMinimality_IndependentReconstructionProbe.md`), two frontier models from different families were given the thirteen primitives plus V1/V5 and asked to derive gravity. **Both independently took the commitment rate linear in bandwidth** — which *is* P-Commitment-Linear, so this is convergent support for that postulate, not a challenge to it. What neither run did was separate the rate a front advances from the rate a clock ticks: both set `ν_clock ∝ B` directly. Neither reached `ν_clock ∝ √b`. One noticed the shortfall, proposed `g₀₀ = −(B/B₀)²` to recover its own linear law, and stated it could not reach `√(1 − 2GM/c²R)`. GR-I's front-on-the-null-cone construction gives `N² ~ b`, hence `dτ/dt = √b` and `g₀₀ ~ −b`, which is what yields `g₀₀g_rr ~ −1` — the Schwarzschild relation, the Einstein branch rather than the conformal one, and therefore the factor of two. **The load-bearing step is therefore GR-I §4.2's front-null identification, not the linearity.** Putting the advancing front on the null cone of `ds² = −N²dt² + b⁻¹dx²` with `dx/dt = Γ ∝ b` gives `0 = −N² + b⁻¹b²`, hence `N² ~ b`, `N = √b`; in general `Γ ∝ b^α` gives `N² ~ b^{2α−1}`, so the whole Einstein/Nordström fork lives in that exponent. No outside run made that move or mentioned it. *GR-III §4's forcing of α = 1 was traced separately on 2026-09-04 — see item 12.* *No tier changed. Record-only, flagged for the next gravity pass.* Same probe also found that both runs **postulate** the `1/R` source profile that 026/027 derive from the `N(R)` channel count, and that removing P08 costs the inverse-square law and `G` outright.
10. **The `1/(2π)` in a₀ — OPEN, found 2026-09-04, not previously recorded.** Reading `Paper_028` §6 and `Paper_029` §5.1 line by line, the 1/(2π) in `a₀ = cH₀/(2π)` is **not established as structurally forced** by the papers as written. `Paper_028` §6.3 computes the azimuthal integral and states its value: `∫₀^{2π} dφ/(2π) = 1`. §6.4 then "pulls out" a 1/(2π) that the algebra has already cancelled, and concedes in its own words that the factor's *location* (a separate `dφ/(2π)` measure versus absorption into `Y10`'s `√(3/4π)`) is **conventional**. `Paper_029` §5.1 shows the same cancellation explicitly: its displayed equation carries `(1/2π) · ... · 2π`, leaving no `2π` on the right-hand side, and the sentence immediately after it nonetheless reports the factor as surviving. The 1/(2π) then re-enters at the assembly step, where `N_substrate` is *chosen* (§5.1 calls it "a substrate-level normalization choice") at exactly the value that produces the answer, absorbing the `2/3` polar factor and `G_φ` along the way. **`N_substrate` is fixed nowhere.** `Paper_026`/`Paper_027` fix `κ_V1`, the per-channel V1 envelope coupling, a different normalization doing a different job; neither mentions `N_substrate`. `Paper_027` defers the `2π` forward to `Paper_029` §5.1. `Paper_028` defers both the substrate-source normalization and the order-unity coefficient forward to `Paper_029` §5. `Paper_029` §5.1 calls it a convention. The references form a loop and nothing computes it. **Why this is not bookkeeping.** A measure-normalization convention cannot carry a physical prediction: adopt the unnormalized measure `∫dφ` and the same algebra returns `2π` rather than `1`, moving the "parameter-free" number by `4π²`. A quantity that changes when the bookkeeping is renamed is not predicting anything. **What is unaffected.** `a₀ ~ cH₀`, the horizon-tying, which is the physically substantive claim, stands untouched, as does the `R_H = c/H₀` decoupling surface itself. So does `a₀(z) = cH(z)/(2π)` **as a form**, including the forced exponent of exactly 1, because the evolution follows from `a₀` riding the live horizon and not from the `2π`. The flagship falsifiable prediction is intact. What is suspended is narrower: the "parameter-free, 1/(2π) structurally forced" claim, which is precisely the part this arc identifies as ED-distinctive, since BTFR slope-4 and its tightness are MOND-shared. **Repair routes — updated 2026-09-04, see `Note_a0_TwoPi_RepairRoutes.md`.** The mode-amplitude route named here originally is **CLOSED as a negative**: the `∫dφ` is a coarse-grained stand-in for a sum over discrete channels (P07), and P04 makes bandwidth additive *across* channels, so the substrate object is a sum, not an angular density — converting it introduces a channel count `N_az`, never a bare 1/(2π). The route asks the primitives for the opposite of what they say. **A materially better route is now open.** `Paper_BH_Thermal2Pi_EntropyCoefficient` derives a genuinely physical `2π`: the no-conical-defect smoothness condition on ED's near-horizon Rindler `b`-profile, which nothing cancels. An accelerating chain has a Rindler horizon with `κ_a = a/c`; the cosmic decoupling surface is de Sitter-type with `T_H = κ_H/(2π) = H₀/(2π)`; and `κ_a = T_H` returns `a₀ = cH₀/(2π)` **exactly**, with the `2π` supplied by the horizon's own smoothness. If grounded, the `2π` in `a₀` becomes **the same `2π`** as in `T = κ/(2π)` — one fact appearing twice, the untunability structure `c_T = c` / `α₂ = 0` already carries. **SHARPENED 2026-09-04 by external audit — the normalization does *two* jobs, not one.** The flawed chain was put to two frontier models as a blind calibration test (known answer, withheld). **Both independently located the defect at the same step**, and one went further than this ledger had. The point missed here: after the azimuthal cancellation the surviving geometric factor is `2/3`, so the displayed integral yields **`(2/3)·cH₀`**, not `cH₀`. Reaching `cH₀/(2π)` therefore requires the §5.1 normalization to do **two separate things** — *erase* the `2/3` **and** *supply* a `1/(2π)` that the algebra had cancelled. `Paper_029` §5.1 does exactly that, in one line: `N_substrate·G_φ⁻¹ = (2/3)(|a|/c)⁻¹` is set precisely so the `2/3` and the `|a|/c` divide out. *Fairness note: absorbing an O(1) geometric factor into a strain-to-acceleration normalization is not illegitimate in itself — the defect is that the normalization is fixed nowhere, so it can absorb and supply whatever is required. But the audit is right that the honest output of the displayed integral is `2/3`, and this ledger previously understated how much the normalization was carrying.* **Second audit point, also correct:** the `2/3` presupposes a dipolar `cosθ` in the density that §5.1 does not display — the monopole term against the `cosθ` projector integrates to zero, so the step is sound only given an explicit dipole form. **Third, and it corroborates the repair route:** one model concluded unprompted that the step could only survive given *"a second, independent source of a `1/(2π)` factor — not the same azimuthal normalization already used"*. That is precisely `Note_a0_TwoPi_RepairRoutes.md` §3's Route 2 (the horizon smoothness `2π`), identified independently and without sight of it. **Matching condition advanced 2026-09-04 (note §4b), not closed.** The *horizon* half is now grounded, and on a statement made for unrelated reasons in 2026-05: `Paper_028` §3.4 and §7 declare the cosmic surface **statistical, not geometric**, so there is no `b`-gradient to read off it — hence no `κ` is available to it and its only characterisation is a state, which carries the smoothness `2π`. The *chain* half is now a **layer argument** rather than an assertion: a chain's acceleration is a P11 commitment-rate asymmetry, a bare layer-1 count-rate, while `T_H` is a layer-2 object by `Paper_BH_Thermal2Pi` §4b's own finding. The `2π` is then the **coarse-graining seam crossed exactly once**: comparing layer-1 `a` to layer-2 `T_H` keeps one factor, while comparing `T_a` to `T_H` applies the same conversion twice and cancels it, giving the refuted `cH₀`. **The residual debt is one specific thing:** why the comparison is executed at the chain's layer rather than at a common layer. The offered reason — the chain is what responds, so the response runs in the chain's own machinery, while the horizon can only present itself coarse-grained because it is statistical by construction — is a reasonable sentence carrying the entire `2π`, and is not a derivation. **A testable pattern falls out:** if the `2π` is a layer-crossing factor, every ED quantity comparing a bare chain rate to a horizon thermal state should carry exactly one, and like-layer comparisons none. Unchecked across the corpus. **The whole remaining debt is the matching condition:** `κ_a = T_H` equates a surface gravity with a temperature, dimensionally clean in natural units but asymmetric and unmotivated, and the *symmetric* condition `T_a = T_H` has the `2π` cancel and gives `a₀ = cH₀`, wrong by the factor at issue. A criterion justified only by landing the number is numerology; it must be argued from the substrate or abandoned. Tier ceiling if it closes: *structural given the coarse-grained horizon*, inheriting `Paper_BH_Thermal2Pi` §4b — not substrate-native. Until then the honest label on `N_substrate` is **P**. *Two process notes. `Paper_029` carried **no audit table**, which the paper-writing checklist §1 requires; a P/A/I/D pass would have forced a label onto `N_substrate` and surfaced this at drafting time. **FIXED 2026-09-04: §2.5 added, fifteen labelled rows.** It records `N_substrate` as **P** in the paper's own words, the azimuthal factor as **DISPUTED**, `a₀ = cH₀/(2π)` as **Postulated/disputed**, and — separately — `a₀ ~ cH₀` as **D** and `a₀(z)` with its forced exponent as **D**, so the surviving claims are visible alongside the suspended one. It also flags row 5 (the kinematic-dipole analogy) as an **A** row the checklist does not permit outside verdict framing. And this was found by tracing a pointer, not by re-deriving anything: the papers' own equations say it.*

### Honest arc-state
The gravity line is the corpus flagship and it hangs together at the tier it claims. **The GR keystone (#1) is closed** in the honest sense: GR-I derives the weak-field Einstein metric + factor-of-two (conditional on the single postulate P-Commitment-Linear, which GR-III then *forces* from the P04 band law, modulo a named premise); GR-II fixes the class as **khronometric** (Lovelock + mode count, structural) with `c_T=c` and universal Lorentz-violation as F-independent shields; GR-III builds and runs the dynamical rule (Newtonian fixed point, `r_s∝M`, emergent frozen horizon, area law, Hawking scaling, `c_s=c` derived); and GR-IV closes the preferred-frame kill-switch **favorably** — **α₂=0 exactly** (both cones luminal, literature-verified) and `α₁=−4λ_local≲4×10⁻⁹³` (≥70 orders safe) because commitment is Zeno-forced sparse — while keeping it a live test. The **α₂=0 and `c_GW=c` are one causal-cone fact** (GR-IV §3), which is the load-bearing inseparability the untunability reading leans on. The **MOND/dark-matter line stands**: `G=c³ℓ_P²/ℏ` (form-derived, value-inherited), `a₀=cH₀/2π` (form-derived), the Combination Rule and BTFR slope-4, with **KM-I** unifying them as the khronon's deep field (single hinge "forced-given-030," lensing/ghost/PPN checks passing — *the standing result, not to be reinvented*), and **QuadraticStrain** sharpening 030 by recasting MOND as the off-diagonal interference cross-term (discharging P14 to one strain-reading postulate; residual = the constructive sign, now at measured tier). The honest BTFR distinction holds — slope-4 + tightness are MOND-shared; only the a₀ *normalization* is ED-distinctive. **KM-II** reduces the clusters/CMB debt to one sequestered regulator dial under five filters (orthogonality theorem clean) and pins `𝒲₀=−24π²Ω_Λ` at inherited tier with the ab-initio integral an open number-to-miss. The emergent metric is correctly held as **kinematic/static-linear** (the nonlinear regime is the distinctive/falsifiable frontier, OPEN), and exactly-3 as derived-conditional-on-the-holographic-count, not a forcing. **The propagation debt that dominated this folder is now closed:** the flagship a₀(z) evolution bet and its ~30σ 2026 confirmation, which had lived in only one paper (031 §8.8), were propagated on 2026-07-29 to 029 (new §8.8), 037 (preamble item 6), 038 §3.3 CO-3, Predictions_and_Falsifiers, 22_Ways #1, and Paper_101 (promoted to Tier-1 weapon row 2) — see Staleness #1 for the full list. What remains is the *lower-priority* second pass: the synthesis papers (KM-I, KM-II, One-Field, PhilPapers) still cite a₀ = cH₀/2π without the evolution confrontation. **Defensible headline:** *the GR keystone is closed (khronometric, α₂=0 exact, favorable preferred-frame kill-switch); Newton's G and the MOND scale are form-derived / value-inherited with KM-I the standing unification and QuadraticStrain the sharper foundation; Λ is form-identified with a force-vs-stipulate residual; the nonlinear metric and clusters/CMB are the named open frontier; and the confirmed-but-tense a₀(z) evolution has been propagated out of 031 §8.8 across the primary line (2026-07-29), leaving only the synthesis papers as a lower-priority second pass.*
